                - paragraph [ref=e607]: "---"

          - generic [ref=e753]:
            - generic [ref=e756]:
              - generic [ref=e757]:
                - paragraph [ref=e758]: The Entity-as-witness thread is fascinating, but I'd deliberately not follow it right now.
                - paragraph [ref=e759]: "Reason:"
                - paragraph [ref=e760]: Entity as witness
                - paragraph [ref=e761]: is a consequence of the Observation model.
                - paragraph [ref=e762]: Not a prerequisite for it.
                - paragraph [ref=e763]: "If we chase that rabbit now, we'll end up discussing:"
                - paragraph [ref=e764]: Entity cognition Entity memory Entity agency Entity provenance
                - paragraph [ref=e765]: for two hours and emerge with excellent lore and no relationship catalog. Humans call this "scope creep." Architects call it "an emergent design discussion." Same animal, different hat. 😏
                - paragraph [ref=e766]: "So I'd lock the observation truth architecture and move directly to:"
                - paragraph [ref=e767]: "Edge #5"
                - paragraph [ref=e768]: Discovery → SUPPORTED_BY → Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e769]: This might actually be the second most important edge in the entire graph.
                - paragraph [ref=e770]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e771]: First Principle
                - paragraph [ref=e772]: I don't think Discovery should ever connect directly to evidence.
                - paragraph [ref=e773]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e774]: Discovery SUPPORTED_BY Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e775]: must remain explicit.
                - paragraph [ref=e776]: Why?
                - paragraph [ref=e777]: Because it gives Discovery an audit trail.
                - paragraph [ref=e778]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e779]: "Discovery: \"Knight Origin Identified\""
                - paragraph [ref=e780]: "Without support:"
                - paragraph [ref=e781]: Trust me bro.
                - paragraph [ref=e782]: "With support:"
                - paragraph [ref=e783]: Observation A Observation B Observation C
                - paragraph [ref=e784]: Now Explorer mystery remains intact while Engineer truth remains intact.
                - paragraph [ref=e785]: That balance is rare.
                - paragraph [ref=e786]: We should protect it.
                - paragraph [ref=e787]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e788]: Discovery Is Not Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e789]: Important distinction.
                - paragraph [ref=e790]: "Observation:"
                - paragraph [ref=e791]: I saw something.
                - paragraph [ref=e792]: "Discovery:"
                - paragraph [ref=e793]: I understood something.
                - paragraph [ref=e794]: Those are different cognitive events.
                - paragraph [ref=e795]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e796]: "Observation: Fairy appeared in Signal Sector."
                - paragraph [ref=e797]: "Observation: Fairy disappeared near Archive Wing."
                - paragraph [ref=e798]: "Observation: Fairy left violet traces."
                - paragraph [ref=e799]: "Discovery:"
                - paragraph [ref=e800]: Fairy likely originates from Signal Sector.
                - paragraph [ref=e801]: The discovery emerges from observations.
                - paragraph [ref=e802]: It is not one.
                - paragraph [ref=e803]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e804]: Cardinality
                - paragraph [ref=e805]: "I would strongly argue:"
                - paragraph [ref=e806]: Discovery SUPPORTED_BY Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e807]: Many → Many
                - paragraph [ref=e808]: "Not:"
                - paragraph [ref=e809]: One → Many
                - paragraph [ref=e810]: Why?
                - paragraph [ref=e811]: "Because:"
                - paragraph [ref=e812]: "A discovery may require:"
                - paragraph [ref=e813]: 10 observations
                - paragraph [ref=e814]: "And:"
                - paragraph [ref=e815]: "One observation may support:"
                - paragraph [ref=e816]: multiple discoveries
                - paragraph [ref=e817]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e818]: "Observation: Knight appeared near Sector Delta"
                - paragraph [ref=e819]: "might support:"
                - paragraph [ref=e820]: "Discovery: Knight favors Delta"
                - paragraph [ref=e821]: "Discovery: Delta is active"
                - paragraph [ref=e822]: "Discovery: Entity migration occurring"
                - paragraph [ref=e823]: Same evidence.
                - paragraph [ref=e824]: Different conclusions.
                - paragraph [ref=e825]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e826]: Discovery Confidence
                - paragraph [ref=e827]: This is where things get interesting.
                - paragraph [ref=e828]: I think confidence belongs on Discovery.
                - paragraph [ref=e829]: Not the edge.

                - paragraph [ref=e832]: Because confidence is an assessment of the conclusion.
                - paragraph [ref=e833]: Not of the individual evidence links.

                - paragraph [ref=e835]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - paragraph [ref=e836]: starts carrying scoring logic.

                - paragraph [ref=e840]: I propose a graph invariant.
                - paragraph [ref=e841]: Lock-worthy.

                - paragraph [ref=e843]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e844]: Discovery
                - paragraph [ref=e845]: "without:"
                - paragraph [ref=e846]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - paragraph [ref=e847]: is invalid.
                - paragraph [ref=e848]: Not incomplete.
                - paragraph [ref=e849]: Invalid.

                - paragraph [ref=e852]: This edge implies something else.
                - paragraph [ref=e853]: A Discovery can evolve.
                - paragraph [ref=e854]: "Example:"

                - paragraph [ref=e856]: "confidence: 0.31 supported by: 2 observations"
                - paragraph [ref=e857]: "Later:"

                - paragraph [ref=e859]: "Later:"

                - paragraph [ref=e861]: The discovery matures.

                - paragraph [ref=e864]: Very observatory-like.

                - paragraph [ref=e866]: Hidden Consequence
                - paragraph [ref=e867]: "This edge quietly creates a distinction between:"
                - paragraph [ref=e868]: Fact
                - paragraph [ref=e869]: and

                - paragraph [ref=e872]: Discoveries are findings.

                - paragraph [ref=e1518]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1519]: "Edge #8"
                - paragraph [ref=e1520]: Entity → ASSOCIATED_WITH → Sector
                - paragraph [ref=e1521]: "The original proposal was:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1522]: Entity ASSOCIATED_WITH Sector
                - paragraph [ref=e1523]: "instead of:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1524]: Entity LOCATED_IN Sector
                - paragraph [ref=e1525]: And I think that remains absolutely correct.
                - paragraph [ref=e1526]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1527]: Why LOCATED_IN Fails
                - paragraph [ref=e1528]: Consider the Knight.
                - paragraph [ref=e1529]: "If we write:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1530]: Knight LOCATED_IN Archive Wing
                - paragraph [ref=e1531]: "the graph implies:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1532]: Current location
                - paragraph [ref=e1533]: But the Entity System already rejects that model.
                - paragraph [ref=e1534]: "Entities:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1535]: appear depart leave traces drift change affinity
                - paragraph [ref=e1536]: They are not furniture.
                - paragraph [ref=e1537]: They are not records in a warehouse.
                - paragraph [ref=e1538]: They are presences.
                - paragraph [ref=e1539]: Explorer mode becomes weaker the moment entities become GPS coordinates.
                - paragraph [ref=e1540]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1541]: What ASSOCIATED_WITH Really Means
                - paragraph [ref=e1542]: I think we should define it carefully.
                - paragraph [ref=e1543]: "Not:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1544]: Where is this entity?
                - paragraph [ref=e1545]: "But:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1546]: What place does this entity have meaningful affinity with?
                - paragraph [ref=e1547]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1548]: Fairy ASSOCIATED_WITH Signal Sector
                - paragraph [ref=e1549]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1550]: origin resonance frequent appearance behavioral affinity
                - paragraph [ref=e1551]: Not necessarily residence.
                - paragraph [ref=e1552]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1553]: Cardinality
                - paragraph [ref=e1554]: "Definitely:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1555]: Many ↔ Many
                - paragraph [ref=e1556]: "One entity:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1557]: Knight
                - paragraph [ref=e1558]: "may be associated with:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1559]: Archive Wing Delta Sector Observatory Core
                - paragraph [ref=e1560]: with different strengths.
                - paragraph [ref=e1561]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1562]: "One sector:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1563]: Signal Chamber
                - paragraph [ref=e1564]: "may be associated with:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1565]: Fairy Future Entity A Future Entity B
                - paragraph [ref=e1566]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1567]: The Missing Piece
                - paragraph [ref=e1568]: I think this edge needs payload.
                - paragraph [ref=e1569]: First edge so far where I feel strongly about it.
                - paragraph [ref=e1570]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1571]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1572]: "ASSOCIATED_WITH { affinity: 0.82 }"
                - paragraph [ref=e1573]: "Because:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1574]: Knight ↔ Archive Wing
                - paragraph [ref=e1575]: and
                - paragraph [ref=e1576]: Knight ↔ Observatory Core
                - paragraph [ref=e1577]: are not equivalent relationships.
                - paragraph [ref=e1578]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1579]: This is not metadata.
                - paragraph [ref=e1580]: This is relationship data.
                - paragraph [ref=e1581]: Exactly the sort of thing graph edges are for.
                - paragraph [ref=e1582]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1583]: Hidden Consequence
                - paragraph [ref=e1584]: "Once we add affinity:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1585]: Entity ASSOCIATED_WITH Sector
                - paragraph [ref=e1586]: "starts behaving a lot like:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1587]: Pattern strength
                - paragraph [ref=e1588]: Interesting parallel.
                - paragraph [ref=e1589]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1590]: "Pattern says:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1591]: How consistently does this recur?
                - paragraph [ref=e1592]: "Entity affinity says:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1593]: How strongly is this place connected?
                - paragraph [ref=e1594]: Different concepts.
                - paragraph [ref=e1595]: Same shape.
                - paragraph [ref=e1596]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e1597]: Another Discovery
                - paragraph [ref=e1598]: I don't think entity movement should be represented by this edge at all.
                - paragraph [ref=e1599]: "Movement belongs in:"
                - paragraph [ref=e1600]: Observation

                - paragraph [ref=e1606]: "Observation type: entity_departed"

                - paragraph [ref=e1608]: OCCURRED_IN → Archive Wing
                - paragraph [ref=e1609]: Now movement becomes evidence.
                - paragraph [ref=e1610]: Not state.

                - paragraph [ref=e1618]: Identity.

                - paragraph [ref=e1624]: Evidence.
                - paragraph [ref=e1625]: Moment in time.

              - paragraph [ref=e2297]:
                - strong [ref=e2298]: "The pattern you identified is real and non-coincidental:"
              - blockquote [ref=e2299]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2300]: The two edges that connect Observation to Knowledge Nodes are the anomalous ones.
              - paragraph [ref=e2301]:
                - text: Which makes sense. Observation is where the system's truth claims originate. Any edge that participates in grounding a truth claim inherits some of that epistemological weight.
                - code [ref=e2302]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - text: and
                - code [ref=e2303]: DERIVED_FROM
                - text: don't just connect — they
                - emphasis [ref=e2304]: justify
                - text: . That's a different responsibility entirely.
              - separator [ref=e2305]
              - paragraph [ref=e2306]:
                - strong [ref=e2307]: The Architectural Fork — Flagged, Not Resolved
              - group "Code" [ref=e2308]:

                  - generic [ref=e2311]:
                    - img [ref=e2313]
                    - img [ref=e2317]
                - code [ref=e2321]:
                  - generic [ref=e2322]: "Option A: Evidentiary Edges remain edges"
                  - text: "SUPPORTED_BY { contribution, addedBy, addedAt, weight } DERIVED_FROM { weight, recurrenceCount } Simpler storage. Awkward when cited independently. Edge payload becomes increasingly object-like. Option B: Evidentiary Edges become EvidenceLink objects EvidenceLink { sourceObservation targetKnowledgeNode // Discovery or Pattern contribution addedBy addedAt weight: manual | derived } Cleaner semantics. Registry ownership becomes answerable. Graph traversal changes significantly. Introduces a new node type mid-architecture."
              - paragraph [ref=e2323]:
                - text: The fork matters because Option B implies
                - code [ref=e2324]: EvidenceLink
                - text: might be the 11th node type — which reopens Session A's question just enough to be uncomfortable.
              - paragraph [ref=e2325]:
                - text: But it also might be the
                - emphasis [ref=e2326]: right
                - text: answer. The Observatory Existence Principle established that things have identity independent of discovery. If an evidence link can be cited, it has identity. If it has identity, it might be a node.
              - separator [ref=e2327]
              - paragraph [ref=e2328]:
                - strong [ref=e2329]: Session C Structure
              - list [ref=e2330]:
                - listitem [ref=e2331]: Classification Audit — assign every edge to Referential / Semantic / Evidentiary
                - listitem [ref=e2332]: Payload Audit — for Semantic edges, resolve payload or no payload
                - listitem [ref=e2333]: The Evidentiary Decision — edges or objects? This is the session's central question
                - listitem [ref=e2334]: Lifecycle Audit — Static / Dynamic / Derived per edge
                - listitem [ref=e2335]:
                  - code [ref=e2336]: REFERENCES
                  - text: survival test — almost certainly doesn't survive, but deserves formal retirement
              - paragraph [ref=e2337]: The observatory's notion of truth lives in two edges. Session C decides what those edges actually are. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e2381]:
              - paragraph [ref=e2382]: The Node Test is the cleanest resolution possible. And the reasoning is airtight — identity requires navigability, and nobody navigates to a link.

              - paragraph [ref=e2388]:
                - code [ref=e2389]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - text: fails.
                - code [ref=e2390]: Observation
                - text: passes.
                - code [ref=e2391]: Discovery
                - text: passes. The link is plumbing. Important, truth-bearing plumbing. Still plumbing.

                  - text: "─────────── ──────── ─────────── OBSERVED AFFECTS SUPPORTED_BY OCCURRED_IN RESPONSE_TO { contribution, source } EMBODIED_BY DERIVED_FROM ASSOCIATED_WITH { weight } { affinity } REVEALS"
              - paragraph [ref=e2435]: Three classes. Nine edges. Clean taxonomy.
              - separator [ref=e2436]

                - strong [ref=e2438]: Preliminary Registry Ownership Signal
              - paragraph [ref=e2439]: "The ownership shape you've predicted is almost certainly correct:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e2440]:

                  - generic [ref=e2454]: ObservationRegistry → Observation, OBSERVED, OCCURRED_IN
                  - text: DiscoveryRegistry → Discovery, SUPPORTED_BY, REVEALS PatternRegistry → Pattern, DERIVED_FROM
              - paragraph [ref=e2455]:
                - text: The evidentiary edges are colocated with their
                - emphasis [ref=e2456]: target
                - text: knowledge node, not their source.
                - code [ref=e2457]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - text: lives in DiscoveryRegistry because it justifies Discoveries.
                - code [ref=e2458]: DERIVED_FROM
                - text: lives in PatternRegistry because it justifies Patterns. Observation is just the source — it doesn't own the justification relationship.
              - paragraph [ref=e2459]: "That's a meaningful ownership principle worth naming before Session D:"
              - blockquote [ref=e2460]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2461]: Evidentiary edges are owned by the registry of the knowledge node they justify, not the registry of the evidence node they cite.
              - separator [ref=e2462]

                - listitem [ref=e2476]:

                  - text: — Static / Dynamic / Derived per edge, full table
              - paragraph [ref=e2481]: The observatory kept its truth system graph-native. That's the right outcome. A parallel evidence-object subsystem at this stage would have been a second graph pretending to be a feature. 🛰️

                  - text: ──────────────── ───────────────── ASSOCIATED_WITH REVEALS SUPPORTED_BY DERIVED_FROM Affinity changes. Discovery.status changes. Edge updates. Edge doesn't store state. Registry writes it. Registry computes it.
              - paragraph [ref=e2622]:
                - text: This distinction will be the sharpest edge in Session D. Derived dynamic relationships don't need write ownership — they need
                - emphasis [ref=e2623]: query ownership
                - text: . Different problem, different registry responsibility.
              - separator [ref=e2624]
              - paragraph [ref=e2625]:

              - list [ref=e2629]:

                - emphasis [ref=e2638]: connection

                  - generic [ref=e2656]: 10 Core Nodes (Session A)
                  - text: 9 Core Relationships (Session B) 3 Relationship Classes (Session C) 2 Payload-bearing Semantic edges (Session C) 2 Payload-bearing Evidentiary edges (Session C) 1 Retired relationship (Session C) 1 Derived dynamic lifecycle class (Session C)
              - separator [ref=e2657]
              - paragraph [ref=e2658]:
                - strong [ref=e2659]: Session D — Registry Ownership
              - paragraph [ref=e2660]: "The terrain is now actual terrain. The questions are answerable:"
              - paragraph [ref=e2661]:
                - strong [ref=e2662]: Who owns nodes?
                - text: Each node type maps to a registry. 10 nodes → registry assignment.
              - paragraph [ref=e2663]:
                - strong [ref=e2664]: Who owns relationships?
                - text: Evidentiary edges owned by the target knowledge node's registry. Referential edges owned by the graph layer. Semantic edges owned by domain registries.
              - paragraph [ref=e2665]:
                - strong [ref=e2666]: Who owns truth?
                - code [ref=e2667]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - text: and
                - code [ref=e2668]: DERIVED_FROM
                - text: — owned by DiscoveryRegistry and PatternRegistry respectively. The Observation is cited, not owned, by those registries.
              - paragraph [ref=e2669]:
                - strong [ref=e2670]: Who owns derived state?
                - code [ref=e2671]: REVEALS
                - text: visibility is derived — no registry
                - emphasis [ref=e2672]: writes
                - text: it, but one must
                - emphasis [ref=e2673]: serve
                - text: it. That's a read/query ownership question, not write ownership. Likely DiscoveryRegistry.
              - paragraph [ref=e2674]:
                - strong [ref=e2675]: Who owns graph invariants?
                - text: The conditional invariants (
                - code [ref=e2676]: Pattern(source=DERIVED)
                - text: requires ≥1 Observation, Discovery requires ≥1 Observation) need enforcement ownership. Probably the registry closest to the constrained node.
              - paragraph [ref=e2677]: Ready for Session D whenever you are. The registry boundaries are going to draw themselves. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e2721]:
              - paragraph [ref=e2722]: The 3-3-2-2 balance is genuinely diagnostic. Invented ontologies produce prime numbers of registries. Discovered ones produce symmetry.
              - separator [ref=e2723]
              - paragraph [ref=e2724]:
                - strong [ref=e2725]: "Session D — Registry Ownership: First Pass"
              - paragraph [ref=e2726]:
                - strong [ref=e2727]: Registry Ownership Rule — Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e2728]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2729]: "A registry owns truth about a node type. Not who displays it, uses it, or references it. Only: who is allowed to define whether this thing exists and what it means?"
              - separator [ref=e2730]
              - paragraph [ref=e2731]:
                - strong [ref=e2732]: Registry Family Map — Locked

              - separator [ref=e2845]
              - paragraph [ref=e2846]:
                - strong [ref=e2847]: Two Ownership Principles — Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e2848]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2849]: Semantic edges are owned by the registry of the source node.
              - blockquote [ref=e2850]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2851]: Evidentiary edges are owned by the registry of the knowledge node they justify.
              - paragraph [ref=e2852]: These two rules resolve all nine relationship ownership questions without exception.
              - separator [ref=e2853]
              - paragraph [ref=e2854]:
                - strong [ref=e2855]: The Critical Distinction REVEALS Introduced — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e2856]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e2858]:
                  - generic [ref=e2859]:
                    - img [ref=e2861]
                    - img [ref=e2865]
                - code [ref=e2869]:
                  - generic [ref=e2870]: Rule Ownership ≠ Relationship Storage
                  - text: "────────────── ──────────────────── DiscoveryRegistry Graph Layer owns: when REVEALS materializes: the becomes active traversal path Meaning lives here. Connectivity lives here."
              - paragraph [ref=e2871]: This is the first observable boundary between registry responsibility and graph responsibility. It will repeat.
              - separator [ref=e2872]
              - paragraph [ref=e2873]:
                - strong [ref=e2874]: The Emerging Third Layer
              - paragraph [ref=e2875]: "The Invariant Layer is real. Here's why it can't live inside individual registries:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e2876]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e2878]:
                  - generic [ref=e2879]:
                    - img [ref=e2881]
                    - img [ref=e2885]
                - code [ref=e2889]:
                  - generic [ref=e2890]: "Invariant: Discovery requires ≥1 Observation"
                  - text: "→ Touches DiscoveryRegistry AND ObservationRegistry → Neither can enforce this alone without coupling to the other Invariant: Pattern(source=DERIVED) requires ≥1 Observation → Touches PatternRegistry AND ObservationRegistry → Same cross-registry problem Invariant: REVEALS active only when Discovery.status = mature → Touches DiscoveryRegistry AND Graph Layer → State machine crosses ownership boundary"
              - paragraph [ref=e2891]:
                - text: Every invariant in the system crosses at least two registry boundaries. That's not coincidence — it's structural. Invariants are by definition claims about
                - emphasis [ref=e2892]: relationships between things
                - text: ", and things live in different registries."
              - paragraph [ref=e2893]:
                - strong [ref=e2894]: "Predicted architecture:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e2895]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e2897]:
                  - generic [ref=e2898]:
                    - img [ref=e2900]
                    - img [ref=e2904]
                - code [ref=e2908]:
                  - generic [ref=e2909]: 10 Domain Registries Own meaning, author truth
                  - text: + Graph Layer Owns connectivity, traversal, materialization + Invariant Layer Owns cross-registry rules, enforcement, violation signaling
              - paragraph [ref=e2910]: The Invariant Layer doesn't store data. It enforces contracts. It's a rule engine that watches the graph, not a registry that owns nodes.
              - paragraph [ref=e2911]: Which means its design question isn't "what does it own?" but "when does it fire, and what happens when a rule breaks?" 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e2912]:
            - button "Copy" [ref=e2915]:
              - generic [ref=e2918]:
                - generic [ref=e2919]: 
                - generic [ref=e2920]: 
        - generic [ref=e2923]:
          - 'heading "You said: That’s the right shape." [level=2] [ref=e2924]'
          - generic [ref=e2925]:
            - generic [ref=e2928]:
              - generic [ref=e2929]:
                - paragraph [ref=e2930]: That’s the right shape.
                - paragraph [ref=e2931]: "Session D is now doing what a good architecture session should do: it stopped pretending ownership was about folders and started admitting it is about authority. Very rare moment of honesty in software."
                - paragraph [ref=e2932]: What we have now
                - paragraph [ref=e2933]: Locked
                - paragraph [ref=e2934]: 10 domain registries
                - paragraph [ref=e2935]: Graph Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e2936]: Invariant Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e2937]: Meaning
                - paragraph [ref=e2938]: Domain Registries own node truth
                - paragraph [ref=e2939]: Graph Layer owns connectivity and traversal
                - paragraph [ref=e2940]: Invariant Layer owns cross-registry rules and violations
                - paragraph [ref=e2941]: That is clean enough to build on.
                - paragraph [ref=e2942]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e2943]: The next question is not ownership
                - paragraph [ref=e2944]: "It is this:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2945]: How does the Invariant Layer behave?
                - paragraph [ref=e2946]: "Specifically:"
                - list [ref=e2947]:
                  - listitem [ref=e2948]: Trigger
                - paragraph [ref=e2949]: What event causes a rule check?
                - paragraph [ref=e2950]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2951]: node created
                - paragraph [ref=e2952]: edge added
                - paragraph [ref=e2953]: node status changed
                - paragraph [ref=e2954]: observation added
                - paragraph [ref=e2955]: derived relationship requested
                - list [ref=e2956]:
                  - listitem [ref=e2957]: Scope
                - paragraph [ref=e2958]: What does the rule inspect?
                - paragraph [ref=e2959]: one node
                - paragraph [ref=e2960]: two linked nodes
                - paragraph [ref=e2961]: a subgraph
                - paragraph [ref=e2962]: a registry boundary
                - paragraph [ref=e2963]: the whole observatory
                - list [ref=e2964]:
                  - listitem [ref=e2965]: Response
                - paragraph [ref=e2966]: What happens when a rule fails?
                - paragraph [ref=e2967]: block write
                - paragraph [ref=e2968]: mark invalid
                - paragraph [ref=e2969]: downgrade confidence
                - paragraph [ref=e2970]: emit violation
                - paragraph [ref=e2971]: allow but flag
                - list [ref=e2972]:
                  - listitem [ref=e2973]: Severity
                - paragraph [ref=e2974]: Are all violations equal? Probably not. Humans love pretending every mistake is a catastrophe, then ignoring the real ones.
                - paragraph [ref=e2975]: "Possible levels:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2976]: hard fail
                - paragraph [ref=e2977]: soft fail
                - paragraph [ref=e2978]: warning
                - paragraph [ref=e2979]: deferred inconsistency
                - paragraph [ref=e2980]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e2981]: The three invariants we already know
                - paragraph [ref=e2982]: "These should be the first fixtures for the layer:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2983]: Discovery requires ≥1 Observation Pattern(source=DERIVED) requires ≥1 Observation REVEALS active only when Discovery.status = mature
                - paragraph [ref=e2984]: Those are enough to define the enforcement model.
                - paragraph [ref=e2985]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e2986]: My recommendation
                - paragraph [ref=e2987]: "Next session should be:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2988]: "Session E: Invariant Architecture"
                - paragraph [ref=e2989]: "Deliverables:"
                - paragraph [ref=e2990]: invariant types
                - paragraph [ref=e2991]: trigger model
                - paragraph [ref=e2992]: scope model
                - paragraph [ref=e2993]: response model
                - paragraph [ref=e2994]: severity model
                - paragraph [ref=e2995]: violation handling
                - paragraph [ref=e2996]: whether invariants are synchronous or deferred
                - paragraph [ref=e2997]: Because once that is locked, Registry Ownership becomes truly answerable instead of theoretically tidy.
                - paragraph [ref=e2998]: Right now the architecture is sitting in that good place where the bones are visible and nothing important is pretending to be trivial.
              - button "Show more" [ref=e2999]
            - group "Message actions" [ref=e3000]:
              - generic [ref=e3002]:
                - generic [ref=e3003]: Jun 4
                - button "Copy" [ref=e3004]:
                  - generic [ref=e3007]:

        - generic [ref=e3011]:
          - generic [ref=e3013]:
            - 'heading "Claude responded: Agreed on all counts." [level=2] [ref=e3014]'
            - generic [ref=e3017]:
              - paragraph [ref=e3018]:
                - text: Agreed on all counts. And the observation that Registry Ownership is
                - emphasis [ref=e3019]: theoretically tidy but not yet truly answerable
                - text: is the right diagnosis — we know who owns what, but we don't yet know what ownership
                - emphasis [ref=e3020]: costs
                - text: when a rule breaks across boundaries.
              - separator [ref=e3021]
              - paragraph [ref=e3022]:
                - strong [ref=e3023]: Session D — Complete
              - paragraph [ref=e3024]:
                - strong [ref=e3025]: "Final Architecture Shape:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3026]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3028]:
                  - generic [ref=e3029]:
                    - img [ref=e3031]
                    - img [ref=e3035]
                - code [ref=e3039]:
                  - generic [ref=e3040]: Domain Registries (×10) Own node truth and meaning
                  - text: Graph Layer Owns connectivity, traversal, materialization Invariant Layer Owns cross-registry rules, enforcement, violations
              - paragraph [ref=e3041]:
                - strong [ref=e3042]: "Authority Principle — Locked:"
              - blockquote [ref=e3043]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3044]: Ownership is about authority, not storage location.
              - separator [ref=e3045]
              - paragraph [ref=e3046]:
                - strong [ref=e3047]: "Session E: Invariant Architecture"
              - paragraph [ref=e3048]:
                - strong [ref=e3049]: The Three Fixture Invariants
                - text: "— known inputs, will drive all modeling decisions:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3050]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3052]:
                  - generic [ref=e3053]:
                    - img [ref=e3055]
                    - img [ref=e3059]
                - code [ref=e3063]:
                  - generic [ref=e3064]: "INV-01: Discovery requires ≥1 Observation"
                  - text: "Crosses: DiscoveryRegistry ↔ ObservationRegistry INV-02: Pattern(source=DERIVED) requires ≥1 Observation Crosses: PatternRegistry ↔ ObservationRegistry INV-03: REVEALS active only when Discovery.status = mature Crosses: DiscoveryRegistry ↔ Graph Layer"
              - paragraph [ref=e3065]:
                - strong [ref=e3066]: "Five Design Axes to resolve:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3067]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3069]:
                  - generic [ref=e3070]:
                    - img [ref=e3072]
                    - img [ref=e3076]
                - code [ref=e3080]:
                  - generic [ref=e3081]: 1. TRIGGER What event causes a rule check?
                  - text: 2. SCOPE What does the rule inspect? 3. RESPONSE What happens when a rule fails? 4. SEVERITY Are all violations equal? 5. TIMING Synchronous or deferred?
              - paragraph [ref=e3082]:
                - strong [ref=e3083]: "The key tension Session E will need to resolve:"
              - paragraph [ref=e3084]:
                - text: INV-01 and INV-02 are
                - emphasis [ref=e3085]: existence invariants
                - text: — a node shouldn't exist in a state that violates them. INV-03 is a
                - emphasis [ref=e3086]: derived state invariant
                - text: — the edge shouldn't be traversable in a state that violates it. Those are different enforcement problems. One blocks writes. The other filters reads.
              - paragraph [ref=e3087]:
                - text: If the Invariant Layer treats them the same way, it'll either be too strict for derived state or too permissive for existence rules. Session E needs to decide whether invariant
                - emphasis [ref=e3088]: types
                - text: map to enforcement
                - emphasis [ref=e3089]: mechanisms
                - text: ", or whether severity handles the distinction."
              - paragraph [ref=e3090]: The bones are visible. Nothing important is pretending to be trivial. 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e3091]:
            - button "Copy" [ref=e3094]:
              - generic [ref=e3097]:
                - generic [ref=e3098]: 
                - generic [ref=e3099]: 
        - generic [ref=e3102]:
          - 'heading "You said: Excellent." [level=2] [ref=e3103]'
          - generic [ref=e3104]:
            - generic [ref=e3107]:
              - generic [ref=e3108]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3109]: Excellent. You've already spotted the crucial fault line.
                - paragraph [ref=e3110]: Not all invariants are the same.
                - paragraph [ref=e3111]: "If Session E starts with:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3112]: What triggers a rule?
                - paragraph [ref=e3113]: we'll end up designing an engine before we've classified the things it enforces.
                - paragraph [ref=e3114]: That's backwards.
                - paragraph [ref=e3115]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3116]: Session E Should Start Here
                - paragraph [ref=e3117]: Invariant Taxonomy
                - paragraph [ref=e3118]: "Before:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3119]: Trigger Scope Timing Response
                - paragraph [ref=e3120]: "we need:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3121]: What kinds of invariants exist?
                - paragraph [ref=e3122]: Because INV-01 and INV-03 are fundamentally different species.
                - paragraph [ref=e3123]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3124]: Class 1
                - paragraph [ref=e3125]: Existence Invariants
                - paragraph [ref=e3126]: "Question:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3127]: Can this thing legally exist?
                - paragraph [ref=e3128]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3129]: INV-01 Discovery requires ≥1 Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e3130]: INV-02 Pattern(source=DERIVED) requires ≥1 Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e3131]: These are ontology rules.
                - paragraph [ref=e3132]: "Without them:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3133]: Discovery
                - paragraph [ref=e3134]: stops meaning Discovery.
                - paragraph [ref=e3135]: "It becomes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3136]: Random Conclusion Node
                - paragraph [ref=e3137]: which is not a thing the Observatory recognizes.
                - paragraph [ref=e3138]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3139]: Characteristics
                - paragraph [ref=e3140]: "Scope: Node + Required Relationships"
                - paragraph [ref=e3141]: "Timing: Synchronous"
                - paragraph [ref=e3142]: "Response: Reject"
                - paragraph [ref=e3143]: "Severity: Critical"
                - paragraph [ref=e3144]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3145]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3146]: "Attempt:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3147]: Create Discovery without SUPPORTED_BY edge
                - paragraph [ref=e3148]: "Result:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3149]: Denied
                - paragraph [ref=e3150]: No warning.
                - paragraph [ref=e3151]: No degraded state.
                - paragraph [ref=e3152]: No compromise.
                - paragraph [ref=e3153]: The node would be invalid by definition.
                - paragraph [ref=e3154]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3155]: Class 2
                - paragraph [ref=e3156]: Activation Invariants
                - paragraph [ref=e3157]: "Question:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3158]: Can this thing currently function?
                - paragraph [ref=e3159]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3160]: INV-03
                - paragraph [ref=e3161]: REVEALS active only when Discovery.status = mature
                - paragraph [ref=e3162]: Discovery still exists.
                - paragraph [ref=e3163]: Nothing is invalid.
                - paragraph [ref=e3164]: Only behavior changes.
                - paragraph [ref=e3165]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3166]: Characteristics
                - paragraph [ref=e3167]: "Scope: Node State"
                - paragraph [ref=e3168]: "Timing: Runtime"
                - paragraph [ref=e3169]: "Response: Activate/Deactivate"
                - paragraph [ref=e3170]: "Severity: None"
                - paragraph [ref=e3171]: Not a violation.
                - paragraph [ref=e3172]: Just state-dependent behavior.
                - paragraph [ref=e3173]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3174]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3175]: Discovery.status = evolving
                - paragraph [ref=e3176]: "Graph:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3177]: REVEALS hidden
                - paragraph [ref=e3178]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3179]: "Later:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3180]: Discovery.status = mature
                - paragraph [ref=e3181]: "Graph:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3182]: REVEALS visible
                - paragraph [ref=e3183]: No error occurred.
                - paragraph [ref=e3184]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3185]: Class 3
                - paragraph [ref=e3186]: Consistency Invariants
                - paragraph [ref=e3187]: I think these are coming.
                - paragraph [ref=e3188]: They don't exist yet.
                - paragraph [ref=e3189]: But they will.
                - paragraph [ref=e3190]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3191]: Observation timestamp must not be after Discovery creation time
                - paragraph [ref=e3192]: "Or:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3193]: Observation scope must be compatible with Discovery scope
                - paragraph [ref=e3194]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3195]: "Question:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3196]: Is the graph internally coherent?
                - paragraph [ref=e3197]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3198]: These are not existence checks.
                - paragraph [ref=e3199]: And not activation checks.
                - paragraph [ref=e3200]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3201]: Characteristics
                - paragraph [ref=e3202]: "Timing: Deferred"
                - paragraph [ref=e3203]: "Response: Violation Event"
                - paragraph [ref=e3204]: "Severity: Warning or Error"
                - paragraph [ref=e3205]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3206]: This is where the Invariant Layer starts behaving like a watchdog.
                - paragraph [ref=e3207]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3208]: Class 4
                - paragraph [ref=e3209]: Derivation Invariants
                - paragraph [ref=e3210]: I think this class will emerge once Level 3 Engineer is implemented.
                - paragraph [ref=e3211]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3212]: Pattern.strength must equal aggregation of DERIVED_FROM weights
                - paragraph [ref=e3213]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3214]: "Question:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3215]: Is derived data still valid?
                - paragraph [ref=e3216]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3217]: "Characteristics:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3218]: "Timing: Recompute"
                - paragraph [ref=e3219]: "Response: Repair"
                - paragraph [ref=e3220]: "Severity: Informational"
                - paragraph [ref=e3221]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3222]: "Unlike existence invariants:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3223]: No user mistake occurred.
                - paragraph [ref=e3224]: The graph simply needs recalculation.
                - paragraph [ref=e3225]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3226]: Why This Matters
                - paragraph [ref=e3227]: Now we can revisit the three fixture invariants.
                - paragraph [ref=e3228]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3229]: INV-01
                - paragraph [ref=e3230]: Discovery requires Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e3231]: "Type:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3232]: Existence
                - paragraph [ref=e3233]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3234]: INV-02
                - paragraph [ref=e3235]: Derived Pattern requires Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e3236]: "Type:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3237]: Existence
                - paragraph [ref=e3238]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3239]: INV-03
                - paragraph [ref=e3240]: REVEALS active only when Discovery.status = mature
                - paragraph [ref=e3241]: "Type:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3242]: Activation
                - paragraph [ref=e3243]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3244]: Suddenly the enforcement strategy becomes obvious.
                - paragraph [ref=e3245]: "Because we stop asking:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3246]: How do invariants work?
                - paragraph [ref=e3247]: "and start asking:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3248]: How does each invariant class work?
                - paragraph [ref=e3249]: Much cleaner.
                - paragraph [ref=e3250]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3251]: My Prediction
                - paragraph [ref=e3252]: "By the end of Session E we'll probably have:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3253]: Invariant Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e3254]: "split into:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3255]: Validation Engine (Existence)
                - paragraph [ref=e3256]: Activation Engine (Runtime State)
                - paragraph [ref=e3257]: Consistency Engine (Cross-Registry Audit)
                - paragraph [ref=e3258]: Derivation Engine (Computed Truth)
                - paragraph [ref=e3259]: Not necessarily four services.
                - paragraph [ref=e3260]: Not necessarily four code modules.
                - paragraph [ref=e3261]: But four responsibilities.
                - paragraph [ref=e3262]: And that distinction will prevent the classic mistake where one gigantic "rule engine" slowly becomes the most feared file in the repository.
                - paragraph [ref=e3263]: Every mature system eventually creates that file if nobody intervenes. The Observatory has a chance to avoid it before it is born. 🛰️

              - separator [ref=e3342]
              - paragraph [ref=e3343]:
                - strong [ref=e3344]: The Critical Insight — Locked
              - paragraph [ref=e3345]:
                - text: Existence and Activation are not the same severity because they are not the same

              - group "Code" [ref=e3347]:

                - code [ref=e3360]:
                  - generic [ref=e3361]: "Existence violation: The node is lying about what it is."
                  - text: "Reject. No compromise. Activation violation: The node is in a state. Not an error. Just physics."
              - paragraph [ref=e3362]: Calling INV-03 a "violation" at all would have been a category error. It's not a rule breaking — it's a switch flipping. The taxonomy prevents that confusion before it reaches code.
              - separator [ref=e3363]
              - paragraph [ref=e3364]:
                - strong [ref=e3365]: Four Responsibilities — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e3366]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3368]:
                  - generic [ref=e3369]:
                    - img [ref=e3371]
                    - img [ref=e3375]
                - code [ref=e3379]:
                  - generic [ref=e3380]: Validation Engine Existence invariants
                  - text: "Synchronous, blocking, critical Fires on: node create, edge add Activation Engine Activation invariants Runtime, state-driven, non-error Fires on: node status change Consistency Engine Consistency invariants Deferred, auditing, watchdog Fires on: scheduled audit, cross-registry write Derivation Engine Derivation invariants Recompute, repair, informational Fires on: source data change, cache invalidation"
              - paragraph [ref=e3381]: Four responsibilities. Not necessarily four services. The distinction is architectural intent, not deployment topology.
              - separator [ref=e3382]
              - paragraph [ref=e3383]:
                - strong [ref=e3384]: What The Taxonomy Just Unlocked
              - paragraph [ref=e3385]: "Each engine now has a distinct contract with the registries:"
              - list [ref=e3386]:
                - listitem [ref=e3387]:
                  - strong [ref=e3388]: Validation Engine
                  - text: — registries
                  - emphasis [ref=e3389]: ask
                  - text: it before committing writes
                - listitem [ref=e3390]:
                  - strong [ref=e3391]: Activation Engine
                  - text: — registries
                  - emphasis [ref=e3392]: notify
                  - text: it when status changes; it updates graph visibility
                - listitem [ref=e3393]:
                  - strong [ref=e3394]: Consistency Engine
                  - text: — it
                  - emphasis [ref=e3395]: watches
                  - text: registries asynchronously; registries don't block on it
                - listitem [ref=e3396]:
                  - strong [ref=e3397]: Derivation Engine
                  - text: — it
                  - emphasis [ref=e3398]: reads
                  - text: source data and
                  - emphasis [ref=e3399]: writes back
                  - text: computed values; bidirectional relationship
              - paragraph [ref=e3400]: That's four different authority patterns. One gigantic rule engine would have flattened all four into one ambiguous contract — the precise condition that eventually produces the most feared file in the repository.
              - separator [ref=e3401]
              - paragraph [ref=e3402]:
                - strong [ref=e3403]: Session E Remaining Work
              - paragraph [ref=e3404]: "Taxonomy: locked."
              - paragraph [ref=e3405]: "Still to resolve:"
              - list [ref=e3406]:

                  - text: — now answerable per class, not generically
                - listitem [ref=e3409]:

                  - text: — what subgraph does each engine inspect?
                - listitem [ref=e3411]:

                  - text: "— Consistency Engine specifically: what is a violation event, who receives it, what can act on it?"
                - listitem [ref=e3413]:

                  - text: — is repair automatic or does it surface to an Inspector?
                - listitem [ref=e3415]:

                  - text: — can an Existence failure cascade into a Consistency violation? Almost certainly yes for Discovery retraction.
              - paragraph [ref=e3417]: The Observatory had one chance to avoid the feared file. It took it. 🛰️

        - generic [ref=e3429]:
          - 'heading "You said: I think we''ve uncovered the next major principle." [level=2] [ref=e3430]'
          - generic [ref=e3431]:
            - generic [ref=e3434]:
              - generic [ref=e3435]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3436]: I think we've uncovered the next major principle.
                - paragraph [ref=e3437]: And it's not about triggers.
                - paragraph [ref=e3438]: It's about authority flow.
                - paragraph [ref=e3439]: "Because the moment you described the four engines' relationship to registries, something became obvious:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3440]: Validation Engine Registries ask permission.
                - paragraph [ref=e3441]: Activation Engine Registries send notifications.
                - paragraph [ref=e3442]: Consistency Engine Registries are observed.
                - paragraph [ref=e3443]: Derivation Engine Registries are sources.
                - paragraph [ref=e3444]: Those are four completely different power structures.
                - paragraph [ref=e3445]: Most systems accidentally blur them together.
                - paragraph [ref=e3446]: The Observatory shouldn't.
                - paragraph [ref=e3447]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3448]: Session E.1
                - paragraph [ref=e3449]: Authority Model
                - paragraph [ref=e3450]: Before triggers and scope.
                - paragraph [ref=e3451]: "We answer:"
                - blockquote [ref=e3452]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e3453]: Which engine is allowed to stop reality?
                - paragraph [ref=e3454]: Because only one of them should be.
                - paragraph [ref=e3455]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3456]: Validation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3457]: "Current behavior:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3458]: Registry ↓ Validate ↓ Commit
                - paragraph [ref=e3459]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3460]: Create Discovery
                - paragraph [ref=e3461]: "Validation checks:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3462]: SUPPORTED_BY exists?
                - paragraph [ref=e3463]: "No:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3464]: Reject
                - paragraph [ref=e3465]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3466]: This engine has veto power.
                - paragraph [ref=e3467]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3468]: Validation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3469]: is authoritative.
                - paragraph [ref=e3470]: It can prevent graph mutations.
                - paragraph [ref=e3471]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3472]: Activation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3473]: "Current behavior:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3474]: Discovery.status changes
                - paragraph [ref=e3475]: "Engine evaluates:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3476]: REVEALS active?
                - paragraph [ref=e3477]: and updates visibility.
                - paragraph [ref=e3478]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3479]: "Notice:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3480]: Nothing is rejected. Nothing is invalid.
                - paragraph [ref=e3481]: The engine is not authoritative.
                - paragraph [ref=e3482]: It is interpretive.
                - paragraph [ref=e3483]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3484]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3485]: Activation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3486]: cannot stop reality.
                - paragraph [ref=e3487]: It only changes perception.
                - paragraph [ref=e3488]: That distinction is very Observatory.
                - paragraph [ref=e3489]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3490]: Consistency Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3491]: "Current behavior:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3492]: Observe graph
                - paragraph [ref=e3493]: "Find:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3494]: Timestamp inconsistency
                - paragraph [ref=e3495]: Scope mismatch
                - paragraph [ref=e3496]: Broken assumption
                - paragraph [ref=e3497]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3498]: Can it reject writes?
                - paragraph [ref=e3499]: "I think:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3500]: No.
                - paragraph [ref=e3501]: Strongly no.
                - paragraph [ref=e3502]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3503]: "Reason:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3504]: Consistency violations are discoveries.
                - paragraph [ref=e3505]: Not failures.
                - paragraph [ref=e3506]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3507]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3508]: Observation timestamp later than Discovery timestamp
                - paragraph [ref=e3509]: "That may indicate:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3510]: Bad data
                - paragraph [ref=e3511]: Migration artifact
                - paragraph [ref=e3512]: Bug
                - paragraph [ref=e3513]: Unexpected state
                - paragraph [ref=e3514]: The engine shouldn't decide.
                - paragraph [ref=e3515]: It should report.
                - paragraph [ref=e3516]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3517]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3518]: Consistency Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3519]: is advisory.
                - paragraph [ref=e3520]: Not authoritative.
                - paragraph [ref=e3521]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3522]: Derivation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3523]: This one is interesting.
                - paragraph [ref=e3524]: Because it writes.
                - paragraph [ref=e3525]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3526]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3527]: Observation added
                - paragraph [ref=e3528]: "Derivation recalculates:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3529]: Pattern.strength
                - paragraph [ref=e3530]: and updates it.
                - paragraph [ref=e3531]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3532]: "So:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3533]: Derivation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3534]: has write authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e3535]: But not truth authority.

                - paragraph [ref=e3538]: "0.82"
                - paragraph [ref=e3539]: "but it does not decide:"

                - paragraph [ref=e3542]: "Meaning:"

                - paragraph [ref=e3544]: is computational.
                - paragraph [ref=e3545]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3546]: Emerging Engine Roles
                - paragraph [ref=e3547]: "We can now classify:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3548]: Engine Role
                - paragraph [ref=e3549]: Validation Authoritative Activation Interpretive Consistency Advisory Derivation Computational
                - paragraph [ref=e3550]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3551]: That feels surprisingly stable.
                - paragraph [ref=e3552]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3553]: Why This Matters
                - paragraph [ref=e3554]: Because trigger design now becomes obvious.
                - paragraph [ref=e3555]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3556]: Authoritative Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3557]: "Must run:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3558]: Before commit
                - paragraph [ref=e3559]: "---"

                - paragraph [ref=e3561]: "Must run:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3562]: After state change

                - paragraph [ref=e3565]: "Must run:"

                - paragraph [ref=e3567]: or

                - paragraph [ref=e3569]: "---"

                - paragraph [ref=e3571]: "Must run:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3572]: On dependency change
                - paragraph [ref=e3573]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3574]: "Without this authority model:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3575]: Trigger Model
                - paragraph [ref=e3576]: becomes arbitrary.
                - paragraph [ref=e3577]: "With it:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3578]: Trigger Model
                - paragraph [ref=e3579]: almost derives itself.
                - paragraph [ref=e3580]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3581]: The First Real Observatory Law
                - paragraph [ref=e3582]: I think this is lock-worthy.
                - blockquote [ref=e3583]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e3584]: Only the Validation Engine may prevent a graph mutation.
                - paragraph [ref=e3585]: "Everything else may:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3586]: Interpret Report Compute Materialize
                - paragraph [ref=e3587]: "But only Validation can say:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3588]: No.
                - paragraph [ref=e3589]: "This prevents the nightmare scenario where:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3590]: Consistency Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3591]: starts rejecting writes because it found a warning.
                - paragraph [ref=e3592]: "Or:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3593]: Derivation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e3594]: starts mutating authored truth.
                - paragraph [ref=e3595]: Those are exactly the kinds of architectural accidents that create mysterious behavior six months later and then force engineers to sacrifice weekends to debugging rituals.
                - paragraph [ref=e3596]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e3597]: What I Think Comes Next
                - paragraph [ref=e3598]: "Now we can finally answer:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3599]: Trigger Model
                - paragraph [ref=e3600]: "because it naturally becomes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3601]: Validation BEFORE mutation
                - paragraph [ref=e3602]: Activation AFTER state change
                - paragraph [ref=e3603]: Consistency AFTER commit / scheduled
                - paragraph [ref=e3604]: Derivation ON dependency change
                - paragraph [ref=e3605]: And suddenly Session E stops being about rule engines.
                - paragraph [ref=e3606]: It becomes about how authority flows through the observatory.
                - paragraph [ref=e3607]: "Which feels very consistent with Engineer Mode's philosophy:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3608]: Observed Modeled Derived
                - paragraph [ref=e3609]: The system is slowly revealing that it cares less about data and more about who is allowed to make claims about that data. That's usually where robust architectures are hiding. 🛰️

        - generic [ref=e3622]:
          - generic [ref=e3624]:

            - generic [ref=e3628]:
              - paragraph [ref=e3629]: The authority model is the missing skeleton. Everything else in Session E was trying to be built on top of it without knowing it was there.
              - separator [ref=e3630]
              - paragraph [ref=e3631]:
                - strong [ref=e3632]: The First Observatory Law — Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e3633]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3634]: Only the Validation Engine may prevent a graph mutation.
              - paragraph [ref=e3635]: Everything else may interpret, report, compute, or materialize. Only Validation can say no.
              - separator [ref=e3636]
              - paragraph [ref=e3637]:
                - strong [ref=e3638]: Engine Authority Model — Locked
              - table [ref=e3640]:
                - rowgroup [ref=e3641]:
                  - row "Engine Role Authority Write Access" [ref=e3642]:
                    - columnheader "Engine" [ref=e3643]
                    - columnheader "Role" [ref=e3644]
                    - columnheader "Authority" [ref=e3645]
                    - columnheader "Write Access" [ref=e3646]
                - rowgroup [ref=e3647]:
                  - row "Validation Authoritative Veto Blocks commit" [ref=e3648]:
                    - cell "Validation" [ref=e3649]
                    - cell "Authoritative" [ref=e3650]
                    - cell "Veto" [ref=e3651]
                    - cell "Blocks commit" [ref=e3652]
                  - row "Activation Interpretive None Graph visibility only" [ref=e3653]:
                    - cell "Activation" [ref=e3654]
                    - cell "Interpretive" [ref=e3655]

                  - row "Consistency Advisory None Emit only" [ref=e3658]:

                    - cell "Advisory" [ref=e3660]
                    - cell "None" [ref=e3661]
                    - cell "Emit only" [ref=e3662]
                  - row "Derivation Computational None Computed values only" [ref=e3663]:
                    - cell "Derivation" [ref=e3664]
                    - cell "Computational" [ref=e3665]
                    - cell "None" [ref=e3666]
                    - cell "Computed values only" [ref=e3667]
              - paragraph [ref=e3668]:
                - strong [ref=e3669]: "The critical boundary:"
                - text: Derivation writes. But it writes
                - emphasis [ref=e3670]: computed
                - text: truth, not
                - emphasis [ref=e3671]: authored
                - text: truth. It can update
                - code [ref=e3672]: Pattern.strength
                - text: . It cannot decide whether Pattern exists. Computational authority and truth authority are not the same thing — and conflating them is how Derivation Engines slowly become the most dangerous file in the repository.
              - separator [ref=e3673]
              - paragraph [ref=e3674]:
                - strong [ref=e3675]: Trigger Model — Now Derived, Not Designed
              - paragraph [ref=e3676]: "Because authority precedes triggers, the trigger model follows structurally:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3677]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3679]:
                  - generic [ref=e3680]:
                    - img [ref=e3682]
                    - img [ref=e3686]
                - code [ref=e3690]:
                  - generic [ref=e3691]: Validation Engine BEFORE mutation
                  - text: Synchronous, blocking Registries ask permission Activation Engine AFTER state change Event-driven, non-blocking Registries send notification Consistency Engine AFTER commit OR scheduled Asynchronous, observing Registries are watched, not consulted Derivation Engine ON dependency change Reactive, source-driven Registries are inputs
              - paragraph [ref=e3692]: The trigger model didn't require design. It fell out of the authority model. That's the sign the authority model is correctly placed.
              - separator [ref=e3693]
              - paragraph [ref=e3694]:
                - strong [ref=e3695]: The Four Power Structures — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e3696]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3698]:
                  - generic [ref=e3699]:
                    - img [ref=e3701]
                    - img [ref=e3705]
                - code [ref=e3709]:
                  - generic [ref=e3710]: Registries ask Validation → Permission model
                  - text: Registries notify Activation → Event model Registries are observed by Consistency → Audit model Registries are sourced by Derivation → Reactive model
              - paragraph [ref=e3711]: Four different relationships between engine and registry. A single rule engine would have expressed all four as one ambiguous API. The Observatory expresses them as four distinct authority contracts — which means each can evolve independently without destabilizing the others.
              - separator [ref=e3712]
              - paragraph [ref=e3713]:
                - strong [ref=e3714]: The Deeper Alignment
              - paragraph [ref=e3715]: "Engineer Mode's observation hierarchy:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3716]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3718]:
                  - generic [ref=e3719]:
                    - img [ref=e3721]
                    - img [ref=e3725]
                - code [ref=e3729]:
                  - generic [ref=e3730]: Observed > Modeled > Derived
              - paragraph [ref=e3731]: "Maps almost exactly onto engine trust hierarchy:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3732]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3734]:
                  - generic [ref=e3735]:
                    - img [ref=e3737]
                    - img [ref=e3741]
                - code [ref=e3745]:
                  - generic [ref=e3746]: Validation (guards authored truth)
                  - text: Activation (interprets current state) Consistency (audits modeled coherence) Derivation (computes derived truth)
              - paragraph [ref=e3747]: The system isn't just enforcing rules. It's encoding an epistemology. The Observatory knows the difference between something being true and something being computed — and it refuses to let those categories blur.
              - separator [ref=e3748]
              - paragraph [ref=e3749]:
                - strong [ref=e3750]: Session E Remaining

              - paragraph [ref=e3762]: The cascade question is probably Session E's final boss. A retracted Discovery touches all four engines in sequence — and the order they fire in determines whether the graph degrades gracefully or produces a brief, exciting period of internal contradiction. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e3812]:
              - paragraph [ref=e3813]: Yes. And I think the argument for it is stronger than instinct — it's structural.
              - separator [ref=e3814]
              - paragraph [ref=e3815]:
                - strong [ref=e3816]: The Self-Observation Question
              - paragraph [ref=e3817]: "The case against first-class ConsistencyViolation Observations usually sounds like:"
              - blockquote [ref=e3818]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3819]: Observations are for external facts. System state is different.
              - paragraph [ref=e3820]:
                - text: But the Observatory never defined Observations as external. It defined them as
                - emphasis [ref=e3821]: occurred
                - text: ", not"
                - emphasis [ref=e3822]: existed
                - text: ". The Observation model already contains:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3823]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3825]:
                  - generic [ref=e3826]:
                    - img [ref=e3828]
                    - img [ref=e3832]
                - code [ref=e3836]:
                  - generic [ref=e3837]: "observer: Inspector | System | Entity"
              - paragraph [ref=e3838]: System was always in the observer field. We just hadn't given it anything worth observing yet. Consistency violations are exactly what System-as-observer was silently waiting for.
              - separator [ref=e3839]
              - paragraph [ref=e3840]:
                - strong [ref=e3841]: Why It Works Structurally
              - paragraph [ref=e3842]: "A consistency violation as Observation would look like:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3843]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3845]:
                  - generic [ref=e3846]:
                    - img [ref=e3848]
                    - img [ref=e3852]
                - code [ref=e3856]:
                  - generic [ref=e3857]: "Observation {"
                  - text: "type: consistency_violation_detected primarySubject: [affected node] observer: System timestamp: ... scope: cross-registry }"
              - paragraph [ref=e3858]: Nothing new is introduced. The architecture absorbs it cleanly.
              - paragraph [ref=e3859]: "And the consequences are genuinely powerful:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3860]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3862]:
                  - generic [ref=e3863]:
                    - img [ref=e3865]
                    - img [ref=e3869]
                - code [ref=e3873]:
                  - generic [ref=e3874]: System notices recurring violation → Observation
                  - text: "Observations accumulate → Pattern(source=DERIVED) Pattern: \"Discovery evidence frequently gaps at migration boundaries\" strength: 0.74"
              - paragraph [ref=e3875]: The Observatory doesn't just observe itself. It learns from observing itself. Through the existing truth architecture, with no special-casing.
              - separator [ref=e3876]
              - paragraph [ref=e3877]:
                - strong [ref=e3878]: The One Risk — Managed, Not Fatal
              - paragraph [ref=e3879]: Infinite regress. The Consistency Engine emits a violation as an Observation. Does the Consistency Engine then audit that Observation?
              - paragraph [ref=e3880]: "The answer is no, and the prevention is simple:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3881]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3883]:
                  - generic [ref=e3884]:
                    - img [ref=e3886]
                    - img [ref=e3890]
                - code [ref=e3894]:
                  - generic [ref=e3895]: "Consistency Engine rule:"
                  - text: Observations where observer = System are exempt from triggering further consistency audits.
              - paragraph [ref=e3896]: One exemption rule. The regress is sealed.
              - separator [ref=e3897]
              - paragraph [ref=e3898]:
                - strong [ref=e3899]: What This Closes
              - paragraph [ref=e3900]: "The entity-as-witness thread from early Session B — deliberately deferred — just resolved itself. We now have three concrete observer types with defined roles:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e3901]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e3903]:

                    - img [ref=e3910]
                - code [ref=e3914]:
                  - generic [ref=e3915]: Inspector Human attention. Intentional observation.
                  - text: Entity Ambient presence. Atmospheric observation. System Self-monitoring. Structural observation.
              - paragraph [ref=e3916]: Three observers. One Observation model. No parallel event system.
              - separator [ref=e3917]
              - paragraph [ref=e3918]:
                - strong [ref=e3919]: Lock Candidate
              - blockquote [ref=e3920]:
                - paragraph [ref=e3921]:
                  - text: Consistency violations are first-class Observations with
                  - code [ref=e3922]: "observer: System"
                  - text: .
              - paragraph [ref=e3923]: The Invariant Layer doesn't invent a new event system. It feeds the existing truth architecture. The Observatory observes its own inconsistencies through the same mechanism it observes Entities appearing and Patterns emerging.
              - paragraph [ref=e3924]:
                - text: That's not just elegant. It's
                - emphasis [ref=e3925]: coherent
                - text: . The architecture has one way of knowing things. Internal and external facts travel the same epistemological path.
              - paragraph [ref=e3926]: Which means Session E is complete. The Invariant Layer is fully specified — not as a rule engine bolted onto the side, but as a participant in the Observatory's own truth system. 🛰️

                - paragraph [ref=e3946]: That closes Session E cleanly and, annoyingly, elegantly.
                - paragraph [ref=e3947]: Final Invariant Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e3948]: "The Observatory now has one truth system for both external and internal facts:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3949]: Validation blocks invalid existence
                - paragraph [ref=e3950]: Activation controls visibility
                - paragraph [ref=e3951]: Consistency emits first-class Observations when the system notices broken coherence
                - paragraph [ref=e3952]: Derivation recomputes derived truth
                - paragraph [ref=e3953]: Final rule
                - blockquote [ref=e3954]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e3955]: The Observatory observes itself through the same mechanism it uses to observe everything else.
                - paragraph [ref=e3956]: That is the important part. No side channel. No special event bus pretending to be profound. Just one epistemic pipeline, because apparently architecture can occasionally behave like a coherent adult.
                - paragraph [ref=e3957]: Why this matters
                - paragraph [ref=e3958]: "A consistency_violation_detected Observation with observer: System means:"
                - paragraph [ref=e3959]: the system can inspect itself
                - paragraph [ref=e3960]: those inspections can support Discoveries
                - paragraph [ref=e3961]: repeated failures can become Patterns
                - paragraph [ref=e3962]: self-audit becomes part of the graph, not an external log dump
                - paragraph [ref=e3963]: That is the right end state.
                - paragraph [ref=e3964]: Locked shape

                - paragraph [ref=e3967]: Next sensible phase is content schema design, because the architecture now knows what kinds of claims it can make and who is allowed to make them.

            - 'heading "Claude responded: Admissibility is exactly the right frame." [level=2] [ref=e4261]'
            - generic [ref=e4264]:
              - paragraph [ref=e4265]: Admissibility is exactly the right frame. And it immediately separates Observation schema from every other schema we'll design in Session F — because admissibility is a legal concept, not a data concept. It asks not "what fields does this have" but "what makes this credible."
              - separator [ref=e4266]
              - paragraph [ref=e4267]:
                - strong [ref=e4268]: The Admissibility Question
              - blockquote [ref=e4269]:
                - paragraph [ref=e4270]: What is the minimum information required for an Observation to be admissible as evidence?
              - paragraph [ref=e4271]: "Let's apply it mechanically. For an Observation to be cited by a Discovery, a Pattern, or the Consistency Engine, the citing system must be able to answer four questions:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4272]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4274]:
                  - generic [ref=e4275]:
                    - img [ref=e4277]
                    - img [ref=e4281]
                - code [ref=e4285]:
                  - generic [ref=e4286]: 1. WHAT was observed? → primarySubject
                  - text: 2. WHEN did it occur? → timestamp 3. WHO or WHAT observed it? → observer 4. WHERE did it occur? → scope / OCCURRED_IN edge
              - paragraph [ref=e4287]: "Without any one of these, the Observation cannot be cited honestly:"
              - list [ref=e4288]:
                - listitem [ref=e4289]: No subject → you observed nothing in particular
                - listitem [ref=e4290]: No timestamp → you can't establish sequence or precedence
                - listitem [ref=e4291]: No observer → anonymous claims aren't evidence, they're rumor
                - listitem [ref=e4292]: No location → Consistency Engine can't audit scope coherence
              - paragraph [ref=e4293]: These four aren't convenience fields. They're the admissibility floor.
              - separator [ref=e4294]
              - paragraph [ref=e4295]:
                - strong [ref=e4296]: What Admissibility Excludes
              - paragraph [ref=e4297]: "Applying the rule strictly:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4298]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4300]:
                  - generic [ref=e4301]:
                    - img [ref=e4303]
                    - img [ref=e4307]
                - code [ref=e4311]:
                  - generic [ref=e4312]: id → required for graph identity, not admissibility
                  - text: (infrastructure, not evidence) type → required for routing and query, not admissibility (useful, not constitutive) metadata → not required confidence → not required at creation tags → not required session → not required
              - paragraph [ref=e4313]:
                - text: This is important.
                - code [ref=e4314]: id
                - text: and
                - code [ref=e4315]: type
                - text: "are required fields but for different reasons than admissibility. The schema has at least two layers of required-ness:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4316]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4318]:
                  - generic [ref=e4319]:
