                    - img [ref=e4325]
                - code [ref=e4329]:
                  - generic [ref=e4330]: "Layer 1: Graph existence id, type"
                  - text: "Layer 2: Evidentiary floor primarySubject, timestamp, observer, scope Layer 3: Context everything else"
              - paragraph [ref=e4331]:
                - text: Conflating layers 1 and 2 produces a schema where deleting
                - code [ref=e4332]: timestamp
                - text: feels like a minor refactor. It isn't. It removes admissibility.
              - separator [ref=e4333]
              - paragraph [ref=e4334]:
                - strong [ref=e4335]: The Observer Field Deserves Special Attention
              - group "Code" [ref=e4336]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4338]:

                    - img [ref=e4345]

                  - generic [ref=e4350]: "observer: Inspector | System | Entity"
              - paragraph [ref=e4351]:
                - text: This field is doing more than identifying a source. It's establishing
                - strong [ref=e4352]: trust tier
                - text: "at the point of creation:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4353]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4355]:
                  - generic [ref=e4356]:
                    - img [ref=e4358]
                    - img [ref=e4362]
                - code [ref=e4366]:
                  - generic [ref=e4367]: Inspector Intentional, human-directed observation
                  - text: Highest interpretive authority Can be questioned, not dismissed Entity Ambient, presence-triggered observation Medium authority Atmospheric, not analytical System Structural, consistency-driven observation Authority limited to scope of engine Never interprets, only reports
              - paragraph [ref=e4368]:
                - text: Observer type will determine how downstream systems weight the Observation. A Discovery
                - code [ref=e4369]: SUPPORTED_BY
                - text: an Inspector observation carries different epistemic weight than one supported by a System observation — even if both are admissible.
              - paragraph [ref=e4370]: "This suggests observer shouldn't just be a string or enum. It probably carries:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4371]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4373]:
                  - generic [ref=e4374]:
                    - img [ref=e4376]
                    - img [ref=e4380]
                - code [ref=e4384]:
                  - generic [ref=e4385]: "observer: {"
                  - text: "type: Inspector | System | Entity id: [reference to specific observer] }"
              - paragraph [ref=e4386]: Because "System observed this" is weaker than "Consistency Engine observed this during cross-registry audit at 03:04:32." The specificity is the credibility.
              - separator [ref=e4387]
              - paragraph [ref=e4388]:
                - strong [ref=e4389]: Admissibility Rule — Lock Candidate
              - blockquote [ref=e4390]:
                - paragraph [ref=e4391]: "An Observation is admissible as evidence if and only if it contains: primarySubject, timestamp, observer (typed and identified), and scope."
              - paragraph [ref=e4392]: Observations missing any of these four fields may exist in the graph but cannot be cited by Discovery, Pattern, or Consistency Engine. They are recorded but not credible.
              - paragraph [ref=e4393]: This gives the Validation Engine a precise, testable rule for Observation creation — and gives Engineer Mode a clear answer when it asks why a particular Observation isn't supporting a Discovery it logically should.
              - separator [ref=e4394]
              - paragraph [ref=e4395]:
                - strong [ref=e4396]: What Session F.0 Just Produced
              - group "Code" [ref=e4397]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4399]:
                  - generic [ref=e4400]:
                    - img [ref=e4402]
                    - img [ref=e4406]
                - code [ref=e4410]:
                  - generic [ref=e4411]: Observation Schema — Three Layers
                  - text: "Layer 1: Graph existence id, type Layer 2: Admissibility primarySubject, timestamp, observer { type, id }, scope Layer 3: Context metadata, tags, provenance, visibility, session (all debatable)"
              - paragraph [ref=e4412]: Article 1 of the Observatory Constitution is now draftable. Everything downstream of Observation — Discovery support, Pattern derivation, self-audit, entity witnessing — inherits this floor.
              - paragraph [ref=e4413]: Layers 2 and 3 are the remaining Session F.0 work. Layer 2 may be complete. Layer 3 is where the interesting debates live — particularly provenance, which is the field most likely to contain a hidden architectural decision wearing a data field costume. 🛰️

            - 'heading "Claude responded: The four-layer model is correct." [level=2] [ref=e4460]'
            - generic [ref=e4463]:
              - paragraph [ref=e4464]: "The four-layer model is correct. And the reason provenance deserves its own layer rather than being buried in metadata comes down to one principle you've already established:"
              - blockquote [ref=e4465]:
                - paragraph [ref=e4466]: Trust logic is one of those substances that spreads through a codebase like spilled ink.
              - paragraph [ref=e4467]: Provenance is the containment vessel.
              - separator [ref=e4468]
              - paragraph [ref=e4469]:
                - strong [ref=e4470]: The Orthogonality Argument — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e4471]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4473]:
                  - generic [ref=e4474]:
                    - img [ref=e4476]
                    - img [ref=e4480]
                - code [ref=e4484]:
                  - generic [ref=e4485]: observer → WHO witnessed it
                  - text: provenance → HOW it was witnessed
              - paragraph [ref=e4486]: These are genuinely independent axes. The same Inspector can generate observations through intentional examination OR through an automated session trigger. Same observer type, different provenance, different downstream weight. Collapsing them loses a distinction Engineer Mode will absolutely query against.
              - separator [ref=e4487]
              - paragraph [ref=e4488]:
                - strong [ref=e4489]: The Four-Layer Model — Lock Candidate
              - group "Code" [ref=e4490]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4492]:
                  - generic [ref=e4493]:

                    - img [ref=e4499]
                - code [ref=e4503]:
                  - generic [ref=e4504]: "Observation {"
                  - text: "// Layer 1: Existence id type // Layer 2: Admissibility primarySubject observer { type, id } timestamp scope // Layer 3: Credibility provenance { origin generatedBy collectionMethod } // Layer 4: Interpretation metadata }"
              - paragraph [ref=e4505]:
                - strong [ref=e4506]: "What each layer answers:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4507]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4509]:
                  - generic [ref=e4510]:
                    - img [ref=e4512]
                    - img [ref=e4516]
                - code [ref=e4520]:
                  - generic [ref=e4521]: Existence Can this node legally exist?
                  - text: Admissibility Can this be cited as evidence? Credibility How much should this citation matter? Interpretation What does this mean in context?
              - separator [ref=e4522]
              - paragraph [ref=e4523]:
                - strong [ref=e4524]: The Critical Consequence
              - paragraph [ref=e4525]: "You've identified something that will save the entire evidence architecture:"
              - paragraph [ref=e4526]: "If trust weighting lives on Observation via provenance, then:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4527]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4529]:
                  - generic [ref=e4530]:
                    - img [ref=e4532]
                    - img [ref=e4536]
                - code [ref=e4540]:
                  - generic [ref=e4541]: "SUPPORTED_BY { contribution: 0.0–1.0 }"
              - paragraph [ref=e4542]:
                - text: stays simple.
                - code [ref=e4543]: contribution
                - text: measures
                - emphasis [ref=e4544]: how much
                - text: this observation contributed to a Discovery — not
                - emphasis [ref=e4545]: how trustworthy
                - text: the observation was. Those are different questions that must not share a field.
              - paragraph [ref=e4546]: "Without provenance as a first-class layer:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4547]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4549]:
                  - generic [ref=e4550]:
                    - img [ref=e4552]
                    - img [ref=e4556]
                - code [ref=e4560]:
                  - generic [ref=e4561]: "SUPPORTED_BY {"
                  - text: "contribution observerType // leaking collectionMethod // leaking sourceCredibility // leaking ... }"
              - paragraph [ref=e4562]: Every evidence edge becomes a trust negotiation. The graph becomes unauditable.
              - paragraph [ref=e4563]: With provenance on Observation, every evidence edge stays clean. Trust is assessed once, at the source, and inherited downstream.
              - separator [ref=e4564]
              - paragraph [ref=e4565]:
                - strong [ref=e4566]: Provenance Field Design — Opening Questions
              - paragraph [ref=e4567]: "Before locking the shape of provenance, three questions worth settling:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e4568]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e4570]:
                  - generic [ref=e4571]:
                    - img [ref=e4573]
                    - img [ref=e4577]
                - code [ref=e4581]:
                  - generic [ref=e4582]: 1. Is provenance required for admissibility or only for credibility?
                  - text: "My read: not required for admissibility. An observation without provenance is citable but unweighted. Pattern.strength and Discovery.confidence simply treat it as minimum-credibility evidence. This allows migration artifacts and partial imports to remain admissible without pretending they're fully credentialed. 2. Is collectionMethod an enum or free text? Probably enum. Otherwise Pattern derivation across observations becomes string-matching, which is fragile and unauditable. Candidates: MANUAL | TRIGGERED | AMBIENT | SYSTEM_AUDIT | DERIVED 3. Does generatedBy reference a specific engine instance or just an engine type? For System observations this matters a lot. \"Consistency Engine\" is less specific than \"Consistency Engine / cross-registry audit / INV-01 check\" Engineer Mode will want the latter."
              - paragraph [ref=e4583]:
                - text: The third question is probably where provenance reveals its own hidden depth.
                - code [ref=e4584]: generatedBy
                - text: for System observations may need to reference a specific Invariant Layer operation — which means provenance for System-originated Observations is structurally richer than provenance for Inspector-originated ones.
              - paragraph [ref=e4585]:
                - text: Same field. Different depth. Same pattern as
                - code [ref=e4586]: "Pattern(source: AUTHORED | DERIVED)"
                - text: — one field, conditional structure, first conditional schema we've hit in Session F. 🛰️

              - paragraph [ref=e4743]: Trust localized. Truth composable. Graph sane.

                - paragraph [ref=e4769]: I don't think we should jump to the Work Cluster yet.
                - paragraph [ref=e4770]: There's one thing Observation just exposed that deserves to be locked before we leave Truth Architecture behind.
                - paragraph [ref=e4771]: Because we accidentally discovered a reusable schema pattern.
                - paragraph [ref=e4772]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e4773]: Typed Depth Pattern
                - paragraph [ref=e4774]: "You already spotted it:"
                - paragraph [ref=e4775]: "Pattern { source: AUTHORED | DERIVED }"
                - paragraph [ref=e4776]: "SUPPORTED_BY { source: MANUAL | DERIVED }"
                - paragraph [ref=e4777]: "generatedBy { type: INSPECTOR | ENTITY | ENGINE }"
                - paragraph [ref=e4778]: These are not isolated cases.
                - paragraph [ref=e4779]: "They are all expressing the same structural idea:"
                - blockquote [ref=e4780]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e4781]: A field declares its category first. The category determines what additional structure becomes legal.
                - paragraph [ref=e4782]: That is not inheritance.
                - paragraph [ref=e4783]: Not polymorphism.
                - paragraph [ref=e4784]: Not metadata.
                - paragraph [ref=e4785]: It's something simpler.
                - paragraph [ref=e4786]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e4787]: Typed Depth Rule
                - paragraph [ref=e4788]: Level 1 Discriminator
                - paragraph [ref=e4789]: Level 2 Conditional Structure
                - paragraph [ref=e4790]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e4791]: "generatedBy { type: ENGINE"
                - paragraph [ref=e4792]: "operation }"
                - paragraph [ref=e4793]: Valid.
                - paragraph [ref=e4794]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e4795]: "generatedBy { type: INSPECTOR"
                - paragraph [ref=e4796]: "operation }"
                - paragraph [ref=e4797]: Invalid.
                - paragraph [ref=e4798]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e4799]: "Because:"
                - paragraph [ref=e4800]: operation
                - paragraph [ref=e4801]: "only exists inside:"
                - paragraph [ref=e4802]: ENGINE
                - paragraph [ref=e4803]: depth.
                - paragraph [ref=e4804]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e4805]: Why This Matters
                - paragraph [ref=e4806]: Because I suspect the Work Cluster is about to use it repeatedly.
                - paragraph [ref=e4807]: "Examples I can already see:"

                - paragraph [ref=e4809]: Different platforms.

                - paragraph [ref=e4821]: Lock Candidate

                - paragraph [ref=e4829]: Discriminator required

              - separator [ref=e4963]

              - paragraph [ref=e4982]: Without Schema Law 01 locked, each of those gets designed independently, named inconsistently, and enforced differently. With it locked, every Typed Depth instance in the Work Cluster is a known pattern with known enforcement behavior.

              - separator [ref=e5177]

                - strong [ref=e5179]: The Deployment Test
              - paragraph [ref=e5180]: "Ask what Render and Vercel have in common:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5181]:

                  - text: "Render only: service type, region, cold start behavior Vercel only: project ID, team ID, edge network config Self-hosted only: host, infrastructure type, maintenance owner"
              - paragraph [ref=e5196]:
                - text: A flat
                - code [ref=e5197]: deployment
                - text: field either stores the union of all possible fields — most null for any given project — or stores a string and loses all structure. Neither is acceptable in a schema that just locked Typed Depth as a first-class primitive.
              - separator [ref=e5198]
              - paragraph [ref=e5199]:

                  - generic [ref=e5215]: "deployment?: {"
                  - text: "platform: RENDER | VERCEL | NETLIFY | SELF_HOSTED | NONE url?: string environment: PRODUCTION | STAGING | DEVELOPMENT // RENDER only serviceType?: WEB_SERVICE | STATIC_SITE | BACKGROUND_WORKER region?: string coldStart?: boolean // VERCEL only projectId?: string teamId?: string // SELF_HOSTED only host?: string infrastructure?: string }"
              - paragraph [ref=e5216]:
                - code [ref=e5217]: url
                - text: and
                - code [ref=e5218]: environment
                - text: survive as common fields because every deployed platform has them. Everything else is discriminator-gated.
              - separator [ref=e5219]
              - paragraph [ref=e5220]:
                - strong [ref=e5221]: The More Important Discovery
              - paragraph [ref=e5222]: "Deployment just revealed something about Layer 3 generally:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5223]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5225]:
                  - generic [ref=e5226]:

                - code [ref=e5236]:
                  - generic [ref=e5237]: "Layer 3: External Presence"
                  - text: deployment? → Typed Depth (platform discriminates) repository? → probably Typed Depth (GITHUB | GITLAB | PRIVATE | NONE) links? → definitely Typed Depth (DEMO | DOCS | CASE_STUDY | OTHER)
              - paragraph [ref=e5238]: Every External Presence field is a Typed Depth candidate. Because "external presence" by definition varies by context — and context-varying fields are exactly what Schema Law 01 was designed to handle.
              - paragraph [ref=e5239]: "Links specifically:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5240]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5242]:
                  - generic [ref=e5243]:

                  - generic [ref=e5254]: "links?: [{"
                  - text: "type: DEMO | DOCUMENTATION | CASE_STUDY | WRITEUP | OTHER url: string label?: string // DEMO only requiresAuth?: boolean // CASE_STUDY only audience?: RECRUITER | ENGINEER | GENERAL }]"
              - paragraph [ref=e5255]:
                - text: The
                - code [ref=e5256]: audience
                - text: field on CASE_STUDY links is doing something interesting — it's the first field that directly encodes mode awareness into content data. A case study link aimed at Recruiter mode presents differently than one aimed at Engineer mode. That's not display logic. That's schema-level intent.
              - separator [ref=e5257]
              - paragraph [ref=e5258]:
                - strong [ref=e5259]: Layer 2 — status as Typed Depth
              - paragraph [ref=e5260]: "Your instinct is right. Status is the highest-leverage Typed Depth candidate in the Work Definition layer:"

                - code [ref=e5274]:
                  - generic [ref=e5275]: "status: ACTIVE | DORMANT | COMPLETED | ARCHIVED"
                  - text: "// ACTIVE exposes: lastCommit?: timestamp activeEnvironments?: string[] // ARCHIVED exposes: archivedAt: timestamp archiveReason?: string // DORMANT exposes: dormantSince?: timestamp revivalCondition?: string"
              - paragraph [ref=e5276]: "The mode consequences you identified:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5277]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5279]:
                  - generic [ref=e5280]:
                    - img [ref=e5282]
                    - img [ref=e5286]
                - code [ref=e5290]:
                  - generic [ref=e5291]: "ACTIVE Recruiter: featured | Explorer: discoverable | Engineer: full inspection"
                  - text: "DORMANT Recruiter: context only | Explorer: mystery weight ↑ | Engineer: history available COMPLETED Recruiter: portfolio | Explorer: discoverable | Engineer: read-only ARCHIVED Recruiter: hidden | Explorer: requires discovery | Engineer: accessible via Terminal"
              - paragraph [ref=e5292]: One field. Four states. Three mode behaviors. Schema Law 01 enforces the structure. The modes interpret it. Clean separation.
              - separator [ref=e5293]
              - paragraph [ref=e5294]:
                - strong [ref=e5295]: Project Constitution — Current Draft

                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5298]:

                  - generic [ref=e5310]: "Project {"
                  - text: "// Layer 1: Identity id name summary // Layer 2: Work Definition type: APPLICATION | SYSTEM | LIBRARY | EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM status: ACTIVE | DORMANT | COMPLETED | ARCHIVED → Typed Depth per state // Layer 3: External Presence deployment?: { platform → Typed Depth } repository?: { host → Typed Depth } links?: [{ type → Typed Depth }] // Layer 4: Observatory Meaning // Carried by graph edges, not fields // Layer exists but is nearly empty by design }"
              - paragraph [ref=e5311]: Layer 4 being nearly empty is the right outcome — not a gap. The graph already carries meaning. Fields that duplicate graph edges are the kind of redundancy that produces drift, where the field and the edge eventually disagree and nobody knows which one to trust.
              - separator [ref=e5312]

            - generic [ref=e5372]:
              - paragraph [ref=e5373]: Agreed completely. And Schema Law 02 is the more important lock of this session — not because deployment wasn't interesting, but because the audience field was the first attempt by presentation logic to colonize content schema, and catching it early matters.
              - separator [ref=e5374]
              - paragraph [ref=e5375]:
                - strong [ref=e5376]: Schema Law 02 — Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e5377]:
                - paragraph [ref=e5378]: Content fields describe truth. Presentation fields describe prioritization. Presentation metadata may not live inside domain registries.
              - paragraph [ref=e5379]:
                - strong [ref=e5380]: "The test:"
              - blockquote [ref=e5381]:
                - paragraph [ref=e5382]: Could this field change without changing the truth of the node?
              - group "Code" [ref=e5383]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5385]:
                  - generic [ref=e5386]:
                    - img [ref=e5388]
                    - img [ref=e5392]
                - code [ref=e5396]:
                  - generic [ref=e5397]: Yes → presentation field → belongs outside domain registry
                  - text: No → content field → belongs inside domain registry
              - paragraph [ref=e5398]:
                - strong [ref=e5399]: "Known failures under Schema Law 02:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5400]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5402]:
                  - generic [ref=e5403]:
                    - img [ref=e5405]
                    - img [ref=e5409]
                - code [ref=e5413]:
                  - generic [ref=e5414]: audience → rejected (surfacing preference, not truth)
                  - text: featured → rejected (presentation priority) recommended → rejected (mode preference) highlighted → rejected (display state)
              - paragraph [ref=e5415]:
                - strong [ref=e5416]: "Known survivors:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5417]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5419]:
                  - generic [ref=e5420]:
                    - img [ref=e5422]
                    - img [ref=e5426]
                - code [ref=e5430]:
                  - generic [ref=e5431]: status → changes project truth ✓
                  - text: deployment → changes project truth ✓ type → changes project truth ✓
              - separator [ref=e5432]
              - paragraph [ref=e5433]:
                - strong [ref=e5434]: Project.type — The Next Knife
              - paragraph [ref=e5435]: "The category field question is the right place to look. Here's why type is sharp:"
              - paragraph [ref=e5436]:
                - text: Categories make existence claims. Saying a project is an
                - code [ref=e5437]: APPLICATION
                - text: vs an
                - code [ref=e5438]: EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM
                - text: isn't just labeling — it's asserting something about what the project
                - emphasis [ref=e5439]: is
                - text: ", which determines what fields are"
                - emphasis [ref=e5440]: legal
                - text: ", what relationships"
                - emphasis [ref=e5441]: make sense
                - text: ", and how Engineer Mode"
                - emphasis [ref=e5442]: inspects
                - text: it.
              - paragraph [ref=e5443]: "The stress test for Project.type as Typed Depth:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e5444]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e5446]:
                  - generic [ref=e5447]:
                    - img [ref=e5449]
                    - img [ref=e5453]
                - code [ref=e5457]:
                  - generic [ref=e5458]: APPLICATION → has users, has deployment, has UX surface
                  - text: SYSTEM → has dependencies, has consumers, may have no UI LIBRARY → has API contract, has consumers, no direct users EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM → may have none of the above, exists to generate Observations
              - paragraph [ref=e5459]:
                - text: That last one is the knife.
                - code [ref=e5460]: EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM
                - text: is the only type that exists
                - emphasis [ref=e5461]: primarily to produce evidence
                - text: rather than to ship something. It has a privileged relationship with ObservationRegistry that the other three don't.
              - paragraph [ref=e5462]: "Which raises the real question:"
              - blockquote [ref=e5463]:
                - paragraph [ref=e5464]: Does Project.type determine which registries a Project participates in?
              - paragraph [ref=e5465]:
                - text: If yes —
                - code [ref=e5466]: EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM
                - text: projects are first-class Observatory citizens in a way
                - code [ref=e5467]: APPLICATION
                - text: projects aren't — then type isn't just a Typed Depth discriminator. It's a
                - strong [ref=e5468]: registry participation declaration
                - text: .
              - paragraph [ref=e5469]: That's either a clean architectural feature or the sharpest knife in the room depending on how we handle it.
              - paragraph [ref=e5470]:
                - text: The categories aren't just labels. They're ontological commitments. And
                - code [ref=e5471]: EXPERIMENTAL_PLATFORM
                - text: is about to force a decision about whether Project type can change the project's graph behavior, not just its schema depth. 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e5472]:

                - strong [ref=e5760]: The Collapse Pattern — Now Three Instances

                - text: "The pattern: something"

                  - generic [ref=e5796]: "Schema Law 01: Typed Depth"
                  - text: "A field declares its category first. Category determines what additional structure is legal. Schema Law 02: Truth vs Presentation Content fields describe truth.               - paragraph [ref=e6482]:

                  - generic [ref=e6501]: "Project {"

              - separator [ref=e6502]

              - paragraph [ref=e6505]: "Project schema: complete."
              - paragraph [ref=e6506]: The graph kept stealing responsibilities from fields. Every attempted field that lost turned out to be a relationship, an interpretation, or a presentation concern in disguise. What remains is genuinely irreducible — identity, state, category, and external presence. The rest is edges.
              - paragraph [ref=e6507]: "F.2 next: Experiment and Certification. Both simpler than Project. The conventions are now established — the question is whether either one surfaces a field that challenges the schema laws or whether they inherit cleanly."
              - paragraph [ref=e6508]: Certification is the one I'd watch. It has a relationship with external authority — issuing bodies, expiry dates, credential verification — that Project doesn't have. External authority is a truth claim the graph can't fully carry. That might be the next field that earns its existence honestly. 🛰️

              - paragraph [ref=e6770]:
                - strong [ref=e6771]: "What's absent and why:"

              - paragraph [ref=e6787]: The last three rejections are the External Authority Principle in operation. Internal truth machinery has no jurisdiction over externally-originated claims.

                - strong [ref=e6790]: The New Distinction — Schema Law 07 Forming

                  - text: ───────────────────── ──────────────────────────── Project Certification Experiment Constraint Observation Formed inside the Received from outside the Observatory Observatory Can be questioned Can be recorded, verified, through Observation or expired — not questioned and Discovery through internal evidence
              - paragraph [ref=e6806]: The Observatory can observe that a Certification exists. It cannot produce evidence that changes whether the Certification is valid. That jurisdiction belongs to AWS, Google, or whoever issued it.
              - paragraph [ref=e6807]:
                - strong [ref=e6808]: "Schema Law 07 candidate:"
              - blockquote [ref=e6809]:

              - paragraph [ref=e6818]: That's the Observatory being epistemically honest about the limits of its own authority. It recorded the claim. It didn't make it.

              - paragraph [ref=e6842]: "The Observatory now has two epistemological pipelines:"

              - paragraph [ref=e6858]: They should never merge. A Discovery cannot validate a Certification. A Certification cannot support a Discovery. The pipelines are parallel, not convergent.
              - paragraph [ref=e6859]: That boundary is probably the most important thing Certification contributed to the architecture — not its schema fields, but the epistemological fence it forced the Observatory to draw around itself. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e7020]:
              - paragraph [ref=e7021]: All locked. And the promotion of Law 06 to Architectural Law 01 is correct — it was never just a schema rule. It's been the force governing ontology decisions since Event became Observation.type.
              - separator [ref=e7022]
              - paragraph [ref=e7023]:
                - strong [ref=e7024]: The Evidence vs Authority Distinction — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e7025]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e7027]:
                  - generic [ref=e7028]:
                    - img [ref=e7030]
                    - img [ref=e7034]
                - code [ref=e7038]:
                  - generic [ref=e7039]: Evidence-Originated Knowledge Authority-Originated Knowledge
                  - text: ───────────────────────────── ────────────────────────────── Observation → Discovery Issuer → Certification → Pattern Authority emerges FROM Authority exists BEFORE accumulated evidence the claim arrives Can be questioned through Can only be recorded, internal evidence verified, or expired
              - paragraph [ref=e7040]: This is more precise than internal/external. The internal pipeline doesn't trust itself because it's internal — it trusts itself because it earned the right through evidence. Certification doesn't require that earning. The authority arrives pre-endorsed.
              - separator [ref=e7041]
              - paragraph [ref=e7042]:
                - strong [ref=e7043]: Certification Constitution — Final
              - group "Code" [ref=e7044]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e7046]:
                  - generic [ref=e7047]:

                - code [ref=e7057]:
                  - generic [ref=e7058]: "Certification {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // Interpretation summary? // External Truth issuer: { name: string // \"AWS\", \"Google\" — stable even if URLs change } awardedAt: timestamp expiresAt?: timestamp // Verification (separate concern from issuer identity) verification?: { credentialId?: string verificationUrl?: string } // Observed State (not controlled state) status: ACTIVE | EXPIRED | REVOKED EXPIRED { expiredAt: timestamp } REVOKED { revokedAt: timestamp, revokedReason?: string } }"
              - paragraph [ref=e7059]:
                - text: The
                - code [ref=e7060]: issuer
                - text: /
                - code [ref=e7061]: verification
                - text: split is correct. AWS remains AWS regardless of what happens to their verification URL. Truth and verification are different concerns — we've been separating concerns for thirty sessions, no reason to stop at the last field.
              - separator [ref=e7062]
              - paragraph [ref=e7063]:
                - strong [ref=e7064]: Observed State vs Controlled State — Flagged
              - group "Code" [ref=e7065]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e7067]:
                  - generic [ref=e7068]:
                    - img [ref=e7070]
                    - img [ref=e7074]
                - code [ref=e7078]:
                  - generic [ref=e7079]: "Controlled State: Observatory decides transitions"
                  - text: "Project: ACTIVE → DORMANT (internal decision) Observed State: External authority decides transitions Certification: ACTIVE → REVOKED (external action)"
              - paragraph [ref=e7080]: This is the first schema instance of a pattern the Observatory already handles conceptually — the difference between authored truth and received truth, now appearing inside a state machine. The Observatory doesn't control Certification.status. It watches it.
              - paragraph [ref=e7081]: Not locking yet. But this distinction will almost certainly reappear. Any node with externally-controlled lifecycle will have Observed State. Certification is the first. It probably won't be the last.
              - separator [ref=e7082]
              - paragraph [ref=e7083]:
                - strong [ref=e7084]: Architectural Law 01 — Promoted and Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e7085]:
                - paragraph [ref=e7086]: If information can be represented more honestly as a relationship, the relationship wins.
              - paragraph [ref=e7087]: "Elevated from Schema Law 06 because its jurisdiction spans:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e7088]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e7090]:
                  - generic [ref=e7091]:
                    - img [ref=e7093]
                    - img [ref=e7097]
                - code [ref=e7101]:
                  - generic [ref=e7102]: Ontology Event → Observation.type
                  - text: Relationships EvidenceLink → evidentiary edge Registries meaning → graph edges, not registry fields Schema researchObjective → summary + graph audience → rejected entirely
              - paragraph [ref=e7103]: It's not a schema rule. It's the Observatory's operating philosophy expressed as a law.
              - separator [ref=e7104]
              - paragraph [ref=e7105]:
                - strong [ref=e7106]: Experiment — The Scientific Instrument Hypothesis
              - paragraph [ref=e7107]: "The prediction is almost certainly correct, and the reason is already visible in the ontology:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e7108]:

              - paragraph [ref=e7123]:
                - text: If that's true, Experiment has a constitutional relationship with Observation that no other Work Cluster node has. Not just that Experiments can be observed — but that
                - emphasis [ref=e7124]: producing Observations is the definition of success
                - text: for an Experiment.
              - paragraph [ref=e7125]: "Which raises the question Schema Law 02 will immediately challenge:"
              - blockquote [ref=e7126]:

                - text: Which means Experiment might be the first node whose admissibility constitution explicitly references another node type — specifically Observation. Not as a required edge at creation, but as the declared
                - emphasis [ref=e7132]: purpose

                - paragraph [ref=e7158]: "The tempting definition is:"
                - blockquote [ref=e7159]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e7160]: Experiment exists to produce Observations.
                - paragraph [ref=e7161]: Sounds reasonable.
                - paragraph [ref=e7162]: But let's test it.
                - paragraph [ref=e7163]: Can a Project produce Observations?
                - paragraph [ref=e7164]: Yes.
                - paragraph [ref=e7165]: Can a Sector produce Observations?
                - paragraph [ref=e7166]: Yes.
                - paragraph [ref=e7167]: Can an Entity produce Observations?
                - paragraph [ref=e7168]: Yes.
                - paragraph [ref=e7169]: Can the Consistency Engine produce Observations?
                - paragraph [ref=e7170]: Yes.
                - paragraph [ref=e7171]: So Observation production cannot be the defining characteristic.
                - paragraph [ref=e7172]: Otherwise half the ontology becomes Experiments.
                - paragraph [ref=e7173]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e7174]: The Better Question
                - paragraph [ref=e7175]: "Instead ask:"
                - blockquote [ref=e7176]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e7177]: What is the primary success criterion of an Experiment?
                - paragraph [ref=e7178]: Now things get interesting.
                - paragraph [ref=e7179]: "For a Project:"
                - paragraph [ref=e7180]: Success = delivery
                - paragraph [ref=e7181]: "For a Certification:"
                - paragraph [ref=e7182]: Success = issuance
                - paragraph [ref=e7183]: "For an Experiment:"
                - paragraph [ref=e7184]: Success = learning
                - paragraph [ref=e7185]: Not shipping.
                - paragraph [ref=e7186]: Not adoption.
                - paragraph [ref=e7187]: Not deployment.
                - paragraph [ref=e7188]: Learning.
                - paragraph [ref=e7189]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e7190]: That distinction is structural.
                - paragraph [ref=e7191]: "Because learning in the Observatory has a formal representation:"
                - paragraph [ref=e7192]: Observation ↓ Discovery ↓ Pattern
                - paragraph [ref=e7193]: Meaning Experiment is the first Work node whose success criteria explicitly reference the Truth Layer.
                - paragraph [ref=e7194]: Not because it produces Observations.
                - paragraph [ref=e7195]: Because it is evaluated by them.

              - paragraph [ref=e7377]: Both are Interpretation. But hypothesis has a directional relationship with outcome that summary doesn't. If outcome = CONTRADICTED, the hypothesis was wrong. That relationship is meaningful — but it lives in the graph (hypothesis text → outcome state), not in a separate field type.

                  - generic [ref=e7713]: COMPLETED + CONTRADICTED ✓ ran, learned it was wrong
                  - text: COMPLETED + SUPPORTED ✓ ran, confirmed hypothesis ABANDONED + null ✓ stopped before conclusion ACTIVE + null ✓ still running COMPLETED + null ✗ suspicious — INV-04 ABANDONED + CONTRADICTED ? pressure-test needed
              - separator [ref=e7714]
              - paragraph [ref=e7715]:

                  - generic [ref=e7731]: "INV-04: Experiment.status = COMPLETED"
                  - text: "→ Experiment.outcome must exist Type: Existence Invariant Owner: Validation Engine Crosses: ExperimentRegistry internal INV-05 (candidate): Experiment.status = ABANDONED → Experiment.outcome must be null Status: PRESSURE TEST REQUIRED"
              - paragraph [ref=e7732]: INV-05 deserves the test. Abandoned experiments that still yield learning are real — an experiment stopped early because it disproved the hypothesis violently enough doesn't need to reach COMPLETED to earn CONTRADICTED.
              - paragraph [ref=e7733]: "Possible resolution:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e7734]:

                - code [ref=e7747]:
                  - generic [ref=e7748]: ABANDONED + null ✓ stopped, learned nothing
                  - text: ABANDONED + CONTRADICTED ✓ stopped because it was clearly wrong ABANDONED + SUPPORTED ✗ suspicious — if it was supported, why abandon? ABANDONED + INCONCLUSIVE ✓ stopped, not enough signal
              - paragraph [ref=e7749]: Which means INV-05 probably isn't "outcome must be null on ABANDONED" — it's "SUPPORTED outcome requires COMPLETED status." The invariant is on the SUPPORTED conclusion, not the ABANDONED state.

              - paragraph [ref=e7820]: And a new Invariant Layer customer — domain invariants that live inside ExperimentRegistry rather than crossing registry boundaries. The Validation Engine gets INV-04 and INV-05 without needing cross-registry coordination.

Law 05: Category vs State Category describes enduring nature. State describes current condition. Law 08: States may be transient or terminal."

        - generic [ref=e8274]:
          - generic [ref=e8276]:
            - 'heading "Claude responded: The analysis holds almost entirely." [level=2] [ref=e8277]'
            - generic [ref=e8280]:
              - paragraph [ref=e8281]: The analysis holds almost entirely. One thing to pressure-test and one thing to add.
              - separator [ref=e8282]
              - paragraph [ref=e8283]:
                - strong [ref=e8284]: What's Correct
              - paragraph [ref=e8285]: "The constitutional definition survives scrutiny:"
              - blockquote [ref=e8286]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8287]: A Constraint exists when at least one otherwise-valid decision becomes unavailable, impractical, or disproportionately costly.
              - paragraph [ref=e8288]: The three-word test — unavailable, impractical, costly — is doing real work. It excludes preferences, opinions, and aesthetic dislikes without requiring a lengthy argument. "I dislike loading screens" fails all three. "Render cold starts add 30 seconds to first request" passes "costly" immediately.
              - paragraph [ref=e8289]: The schema sparseness prediction is also correct. Severity, impact, and scope all failed Session C. They don't become more defensible just because we're now inside schema design. Architectural Law 01 already handled them.
              - separator [ref=e8290]
              - paragraph [ref=e8291]:
                - strong [ref=e8292]: The One Thing To Pressure-Test
              - paragraph [ref=e8293]:
                - code [ref=e8294]: type
                - text: as Category with Typed Depth.
              - paragraph [ref=e8295]: "The candidates:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8296]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8298]:
                  - generic [ref=e8299]:
                    - img [ref=e8301]
                    - img [ref=e8305]
                - code [ref=e8309]:
                  - generic [ref=e8310]: TECHNICAL
                  - text: PLATFORM RESOURCE TIME KNOWLEDGE LEGAL
              - paragraph [ref=e8311]:
                - text: "These feel right intuitively. But Schema Law 03 applies: categories influence expectations, not capabilities. So the question is whether these types expose genuinely different"
                - emphasis [ref=e8312]: truth
                - text: in their conditional depth — or just different
                - emphasis [ref=e8313]: presentation
                - text: of the same underlying constraint.
              - paragraph [ref=e8314]: "Test each:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8315]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8317]:
                  - generic [ref=e8318]:
                    - img [ref=e8320]
                    - img [ref=e8324]
                - code [ref=e8328]:
                  - generic [ref=e8329]: "PLATFORM → platform: string"
                  - text: "\"Render\", \"Vercel\", \"MongoDB Atlas\" This is truth. Different platforms impose different restrictions. Survives. TIME → deadline?: timestamp Legitimate truth claim. Time constraints have a boundary condition. Survives. KNOWLEDGE → skillArea?: string \"Kubernetes\", \"WebGL\", \"SMTP configuration\" This is truth. Knowledge constraints are domain-specific. Survives. TECHNICAL → affectedLayer?: string \"Network\", \"Build\", \"Runtime\" Weaker. This sounds like it might be interpretation. Pressure needed. RESOURCE → budget?: number? unit? Interesting. A budget constraint has a quantifiable boundary. But is the number truth or metadata? Pressure needed. LEGAL → jurisdiction?: string \"GDPR\", \"HIPAA\", \"Export Control\" This is truth. Legal constraints are jurisdiction-specific. Survives."
              - paragraph [ref=e8330]:
                - code [ref=e8331]: TECHNICAL
                - text: and
                - code [ref=e8332]: RESOURCE
                - text: are the suspicious ones. Technical constraints might not need depth beyond the name and summary — the affected layer is probably already implied by what AFFECTS. Resource constraints might want a number, but that number's meaning depends entirely on context.
              - paragraph [ref=e8333]:
                - text: "Possible resolution:"
                - code [ref=e8334]: TECHNICAL
                - text: carries no unique depth (the relationship carries consequence), and
                - code [ref=e8335]: RESOURCE
                - text: carries a simple
                - code [ref=e8336]: limit
                - text: field only if it's quantifiable — otherwise it collapses to summary.
              - separator [ref=e8337]
              - paragraph [ref=e8338]:
                - strong [ref=e8339]: The One Thing To Add
              - paragraph [ref=e8340]: "The asymmetry test you applied is worth naming formally:"
              - blockquote [ref=e8341]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8342]: The node survives without the edge. The edge does not survive without the node.
              - paragraph [ref=e8343]: This is the Node Test from Session C, but applied in both directions. You've been running it implicitly throughout — it should probably be explicit in the spec, because it's the clearest possible demonstration of why Constraint is a node rather than a relationship property.
              - separator [ref=e8344]
              - paragraph [ref=e8345]:
                - strong [ref=e8346]: Constraint Constitution — Near-Final
              - group "Code" [ref=e8347]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8349]:
                  - generic [ref=e8350]:
                    - img [ref=e8352]
                    - img [ref=e8356]
                - code [ref=e8360]:
                  - generic [ref=e8361]: "Constraint {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // Interpretation summary // Truth: Category type: TECHNICAL | PLATFORM | RESOURCE | TIME | KNOWLEDGE | LEGAL PLATFORM { platform: string } TIME { deadline?: timestamp } KNOWLEDGE { skillArea?: string } LEGAL { jurisdiction?: string } RESOURCE { limit?: string } // quantifiable boundary if exists TECHNICAL { } // no unique depth — graph carries consequence }"
              - separator [ref=e8362]
              - paragraph [ref=e8363]:
                - strong [ref=e8364]: What Constraint Is About To Prove
              - paragraph [ref=e8365]:
                - text: If the final schema ends up this sparse, Constraint becomes the strongest possible demonstration of Architectural Law 01 in the entire Observatory. Not because it's simple — because it's
                - emphasis [ref=e8366]: honest
                - text: ". It knows exactly what it is: a named restriction on the decision space. Everything else — severity, impact, scope, priority — belongs to the things it affects, not to itself."
              - paragraph [ref=e8367]: A node that wins by refusing to know more than it actually knows is rarer than it should be.
              - paragraph [ref=e8368]:
                - text: Tradeoff is next, and the prediction is right — authored reasoning is where schema design gets philosophically uncomfortable. Tradeoff has to represent
                - emphasis [ref=e8369]: why a decision was made
                - text: without becoming a philosophy textbook, a justification engine, or a presentation layer in disguise.
              - paragraph [ref=e8370]: That's a harder problem than Constraint, which simply had to resist accumulating fields it didn't own. Tradeoff has to actively carry reasoning. The question is how much reasoning a schema field can honestly hold before it should have been a graph relationship instead. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e8420]:
              - paragraph [ref=e8421]: Locked on Constraint. The TECHNICAL category terminating without depth is the right outcome — and worth making explicit as a schema principle.
              - separator [ref=e8422]
              - paragraph [ref=e8423]:
                - strong [ref=e8424]: Typed Depth — Clarified
              - paragraph [ref=e8425]: "The TECHNICAL finding upgrades Schema Law 01 slightly:"
              - blockquote [ref=e8426]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8427]: A discriminator permits conditional subfields. It does not require them.
              - group "Code" [ref=e8428]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8430]:
                  - generic [ref=e8431]:

                - code [ref=e8441]:
                  - generic [ref=e8442]: "Category exists for two reasons:"
                  - text: 1. To gate subfields (PLATFORM, TIME, KNOWLEDGE, LEGAL, RESOURCE) 2. To distinguish meaning (TECHNICAL — the name IS the information)
              - paragraph [ref=e8443]:
                - text: TECHNICAL doesn't need depth because the category name already does the constitutional work. Adding
                - code [ref=e8444]: affectedLayer
                - text: would be contextualizing truth, not describing it. The customs office was right to reject it.
              - separator [ref=e8445]
              - paragraph [ref=e8446]:
                - strong [ref=e8447]: "Architectural Test: Node Necessity — Formalized"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8448]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8450]:
                  - generic [ref=e8451]:
                    - img [ref=e8453]
                    - img [ref=e8457]
                - code [ref=e8461]:
                  - generic [ref=e8462]: "Forward: Node survives without its edges → node has independent meaning"
                  - text: "Reverse: Edge does not survive without node → edge depends on node for existence"
              - paragraph [ref=e8463]: Both directions must hold for a node to be legitimate. Constraint passes both. Worth cataloging alongside Architectural Law 01 in the final spec.
              - separator [ref=e8464]
              - paragraph [ref=e8465]:
                - strong [ref=e8466]: Constraint Constitution — Final
              - group "Code" [ref=e8467]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8469]:
                  - generic [ref=e8470]:
                    - img [ref=e8472]
                    - img [ref=e8476]
                - code [ref=e8480]:
                  - generic [ref=e8481]: "Constraint {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // Interpretation summary // Truth: Category type: PLATFORM { platform: string } TIME { deadline?: timestamp } KNOWLEDGE { skillArea?: string } LEGAL { jurisdiction?: string } RESOURCE { limit?: string } TECHNICAL { } // category is sufficient }"
              - paragraph [ref=e8482]: Smallest schema in the Work and Decision clusters. Every rejected field — severity, impact, scope, priority — belongs to the relationship or to Tradeoff. Constraint simply names and categorizes the restriction. Everything else is downstream.
              - separator [ref=e8483]
              - paragraph [ref=e8484]:
                - strong [ref=e8485]: The Tradeoff Problem — Framed
              - paragraph [ref=e8486]: "The Decision/Justification distinction you've identified is the real knife:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8487]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8489]:
                  - generic [ref=e8490]:
                    - img [ref=e8492]
                    - img [ref=e8496]
                - code [ref=e8500]:
                  - generic [ref=e8501]: Decision Which path was chosen
                  - text: Answerable with a name and a category Justification Why that path was chosen Requires reasoning — which is dangerous territory
              - paragraph [ref=e8502]: "The danger: justification fields are where schemas quietly become narrative engines. Once a field accepts reasoning as input, it's almost impossible to bound what belongs there and what should be a graph relationship, an Observation, or a Discovery."
              - paragraph [ref=e8503]: "The Observatory has three existing tools for representing reasoning:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8504]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8506]:
                  - generic [ref=e8507]:
                    - img [ref=e8509]
                    - img [ref=e8513]
                - code [ref=e8517]:
                  - generic [ref=e8518]: Graph edges RESPONSE_TO, EMBODIED_BY — structural reasoning
                  - text: Observations witnessed consequences Discoveries understood patterns
              - paragraph [ref=e8519]: "Which means Tradeoff schema has to answer: what reasoning can't be expressed through edges and evidence? That's what earns a field."
              - paragraph [ref=e8520]: "My prediction before we open Tradeoff formally:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8521]:

                  - generic [ref=e8524]:
                    - img [ref=e8526]
                    - img [ref=e8530]
                - code [ref=e8534]:
                  - generic [ref=e8535]: "Survives: chosen path (what was decided)"
                  - text: "rationale (authored reasoning — brief, prose) category (what kind of tradeoff) Rejected: consequence (lives in Observations) alternatives considered (lives in summary or Experiment) success metric (lives in Discoveries) impact score (relationship territory)"
              - paragraph [ref=e8536]:
                - text: The interesting pressure point will be
                - code [ref=e8537]: rationale
                - text: specifically. It's the first field in the entire Observatory that exists to hold
                - emphasis [ref=e8538]: reasoning
                - text: rather than
                - emphasis [ref=e8539]: fact
                - text: — and reasoning is exactly the kind of thing the Observatory has been replacing with evidence chains since Session A.
              - paragraph [ref=e8540]: If rationale survives, it will be because some reasoning is genuinely irretrievable from evidence after the fact. The thought process that led to a decision often leaves no Observations. It just happened, in someone's head, and the only record is what they wrote down.
              - paragraph [ref=e8541]: That might be justification enough. Or the Observatory might decide that unwitnessed reasoning has no place in a truth system.
              - paragraph [ref=e8542]: That question is probably Tradeoff's constitutional moment. 🛰️

It asks "what would be lost without it?"
              - separator [ref=e8594]

              - blockquote [ref=e8597]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8598]: A Tradeoff is the recorded acknowledgment of competing values consciously exchanged.
              - paragraph [ref=e8599]: "Three load-bearing words:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8600]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8602]:
                  - generic [ref=e8603]:

                  - text: acknowledged → the sacrifice was recognized, not accidental consciously → unintended consequences are Observations, not Tradeoffs
              - paragraph [ref=e8615]: "The distinction between accidental consequence and acknowledged exchange is the sharpest constitutional line in the Decision Cluster:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8616]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8618]:
                  - generic [ref=e8619]:
                    - img [ref=e8621]
                    - img [ref=e8625]
                - code [ref=e8629]:
                  - generic [ref=e8630]: Performance accidentally degraded → Observation
                  - text: We accepted degraded performance → Tradeoff
              - paragraph [ref=e8631]: Same outcome. Different epistemological status. The Observatory needs both — and now has formal language to separate them.
              - separator [ref=e8632]
              - paragraph [ref=e8633]:
                - strong [ref=e8634]: The Reconstruction Test — Formalized
              - blockquote [ref=e8635]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8636]: Could a future Inspector reconstruct this honestly from the graph alone?
              - group "Code" [ref=e8637]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8639]:
                  - generic [ref=e8640]:
                    - img [ref=e8642]
                    - img [ref=e8646]
                - code [ref=e8650]:
                  - generic [ref=e8651]: "Passes (graph wins): Fails (field survives):"
                  - text: ──────────────────── ────────────────────── severity rationale impact the rejected alternatives scope the reasoning at decision time consequence why this felt reasonable then
              - paragraph [ref=e8652]:
                - text: Rationale fails the reconstruction test not because it's vague — because it's
                - emphasis [ref=e8653]: unobservable
                - text: . The rejected alternatives leave no graph trace. The reasoning happened in someone's head. The only record is what was written. That's a legitimate gap the graph cannot fill.
              - paragraph [ref=e8654]:
                - text: This is the first field in the Observatory that survives not despite Architectural Law 01 but
                - emphasis [ref=e8655]: because of it
                - text: . The graph honestly cannot represent private reasoning. The field exists to hold what the graph admits it cannot reach.
              - separator [ref=e8656]
              - paragraph [ref=e8657]:
                - strong [ref=e8658]: category — Pressure Test
              - group "Code" [ref=e8659]:

                  - text: SIMPLICITY MAINTAINABILITY COST FLEXIBILITY RELIABILITY
              - paragraph [ref=e8674]: "The test: do these describe the tradeoff, or the value being exchanged?"
              - paragraph [ref=e8675]:
                - text: The value being exchanged — which means they describe what was
                - emphasis [ref=e8676]: at stake
                - text: ", not the decision itself. That's truth. A MAINTAINABILITY tradeoff and a PERFORMANCE tradeoff are constitutionally different. They impose different expectations on Engineer Mode inspection, different relationship patterns with Constraints, different relevance to future Patterns."
              - paragraph [ref=e8677]:
                - text: Category survives. But it probably needs to be a pair rather than a single value — because a Tradeoff exchanges
                - emphasis [ref=e8678]: two
                - text: "things:"

              - paragraph [ref=e8694]: "This makes the exchange explicit rather than implied. A Tradeoff named \"Simplicity over Scale\" now has formal structure:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8695]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8697]:
                  - generic [ref=e8698]:
                    - img [ref=e8700]
                    - img [ref=e8704]
                - code [ref=e8708]:
                  - generic [ref=e8709]: "gained: SIMPLICITY"
                  - text: "sacrificed: FLEXIBILITY"
              - paragraph [ref=e8710]: "Engineer Mode can now query: \"show me all tradeoffs where MAINTAINABILITY was sacrificed\" — and get honest results rather than relying on name parsing."
              - separator [ref=e8711]
              - paragraph [ref=e8712]:
                - strong [ref=e8713]: Tradeoff Constitution — Draft

                  - generic [ref=e8728]: "Tradeoff {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // Interpretation summary // retrospective — what this was rationale? // prospective reasoning — why this felt right // survives Reconstruction Test // Truth: Category category: { gained: PERFORMANCE | SIMPLICITY | COST | FLEXIBILITY | MAINTAINABILITY | RELIABILITY | ... sacrificed: PERFORMANCE | SIMPLICITY | COST | FLEXIBILITY | MAINTAINABILITY | RELIABILITY | ... } // Truth: Confidence (optional — was this exchange comfortable?) // TBD — pressure test needed }"
              - separator [ref=e8729]
              - paragraph [ref=e8730]:
                - strong [ref=e8731]: The One Field Worth Pressure-Testing
              - paragraph [ref=e8732]: "Before locking: does Tradeoff need a confidence or certainty field?"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8733]:

                  - generic [ref=e8736]:

                - code [ref=e8746]:
                  - generic [ref=e8747]: "\"We reluctantly accepted this tradeoff\""
                  - text: vs "This was the obvious right call"
              - paragraph [ref=e8748]: That's not presentation. That's not interpretation. It's an authored judgment about the quality of the exchange — which might matter to Engineer Mode when assessing technical debt or decision quality over time.
              - paragraph [ref=e8749]:
                - text: But it also sounds like it might belong in
                - code [ref=e8750]: rationale
                - text: as prose rather than as a structured field. The question is whether "how comfortable was this decision" is queryable enough to deserve structure — or whether prose captures it honestly enough.
              - separator [ref=e8751]
              - paragraph [ref=e8752]:
                - strong [ref=e8753]: What Tradeoff Just Established
              - paragraph [ref=e8754]: "The Decision Cluster now has three distinct epistemic postures:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e8755]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e8757]:
                  - generic [ref=e8758]:
                    - img [ref=e8760]

                - code [ref=e8768]:
                  - generic [ref=e8769]: Constraint Knows only what restricts. Refuses everything else.
                  - text: "Sparsest node. Graph owns consequence. Tradeoff Knows what was exchanged and why it felt right. Densest node. Holds what graph cannot reach. Pattern TBD — but already partially specified as evidence-grounded Likely sits between: some authored fields, some derived"
              - paragraph [ref=e8770]: The progression from Constraint to Tradeoff is the Observatory's most honest acknowledgment yet that not everything worth knowing is observable. Some things were only ever known by the person in the room when the decision was made.
              - paragraph [ref=e8771]: Tradeoff exists to be that person's testimony. 🛰️

        - generic [ref=e8783]:

          - generic [ref=e8785]:
            - generic [ref=e8788]:
              - generic [ref=e8789]:
                - paragraph [ref=e8790]: I think the pair structure is the breakthrough.
                - paragraph [ref=e8791]: "Because a single-category Tradeoff has the same flaw as a one-sided transaction:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8792]: Performance Tradeoff
                - paragraph [ref=e8793]: Performance compared to what?
                - paragraph [ref=e8794]: The moment you ask that question, the category collapses.
                - paragraph [ref=e8795]: A tradeoff is not a value.
                - paragraph [ref=e8796]: A tradeoff is a relationship between values.
                - paragraph [ref=e8797]: "Which means we've accidentally discovered something interesting:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8798]: Constraint categories describe a node.
                - paragraph [ref=e8799]: Tradeoff categories describe an exchange.
                - paragraph [ref=e8800]: Those are fundamentally different kinds of truth.
                - paragraph [ref=e8801]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8802]: The Exchange Principle
                - paragraph [ref=e8803]: "Constraint:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8804]: type = PLATFORM
                - paragraph [ref=e8805]: "describes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8806]: What kind of thing this is.
                - paragraph [ref=e8807]: "Tradeoff:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8808]: gained = SIMPLICITY sacrificed = FLEXIBILITY
                - paragraph [ref=e8809]: "describes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8810]: What kind of exchange occurred.
                - paragraph [ref=e8811]: That's not a category anymore.
                - paragraph [ref=e8812]: That's structure.
                - paragraph [ref=e8813]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8814]: Schema Law 09 — Candidate
                - blockquote [ref=e8815]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e8816]: When a concept is inherently relational, model both sides of the relationship explicitly.
                - paragraph [ref=e8817]: The Observatory keeps finding hidden one-sided fields and forcing them to admit their missing half.
                - paragraph [ref=e8818]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8819]: Tradeoff gained + sacrificed
                - paragraph [ref=e8820]: Observation observer + subject
                - paragraph [ref=e8821]: Certification issuer + recipient (implicit portfolio owner)
                - paragraph [ref=e8822]: Entity Affinity entity + sector
                - paragraph [ref=e8823]: The architecture consistently prefers complete relationships over partial declarations.
                - paragraph [ref=e8824]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8825]: Confidence Pressure Test
                - paragraph [ref=e8826]: This is the real unresolved question.
                - paragraph [ref=e8827]: "My instinct:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8828]: confidence
                - paragraph [ref=e8829]: fails.
                - paragraph [ref=e8830]: "And it fails for the same reason that:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8831]: severity impact priority
                - paragraph [ref=e8832]: failed earlier.
                - paragraph [ref=e8833]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8834]: "Ask:"
                - blockquote [ref=e8835]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e8836]: What truth does confidence change?
                - paragraph [ref=e8837]: "Suppose:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8838]: "Tradeoff: gained: SIMPLICITY sacrificed: FLEXIBILITY"
                - paragraph [ref=e8839]: "confidence: 0.9"
                - paragraph [ref=e8840]: "versus:"
                - paragraph [ref=e8841]: "confidence: 0.2"
                - paragraph [ref=e8842]: Did the tradeoff change?
                - paragraph [ref=e8843]: No.
                - paragraph [ref=e8844]: The exchange is identical.
                - paragraph [ref=e8845]: Only the author's emotional relationship to it changed.
                - paragraph [ref=e8846]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8847]: That's interpretation.
                - paragraph [ref=e8848]: Not truth.
                - paragraph [ref=e8849]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e8850]: "Now consider:"

                  - code [ref=e9461]: name

                - text: But narrowly. It's not personality, biography, or dialogue. It's the specific designator that distinguishes this Entity from others of the same type.
              - separator [ref=e9462]
              - paragraph [ref=e9463]:
                - strong [ref=e9464]: The Typed Depth Question
              - paragraph [ref=e9465]:
                - text: Does
                - code [ref=e9466]: "type: KNIGHT | FAIRY"
                - text: expose conditional subfields?
              - paragraph [ref=e9467]: "Apply the Constraint TECHNICAL test: does the category need depth, or is the name sufficient?"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9468]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9470]:
                  - generic [ref=e9471]:
                    - img [ref=e9473]
                    - img [ref=e9477]
                - code [ref=e9481]:
                  - generic [ref=e9482]: "KNIGHT → role: GUARDIAN? behavior: PROTECTIVE?"
                  - text: "These are behavioral claims → Observation territory FAIRY → role: SIGNAL? affinity: DISCOVERY? These are relational claims → Edge territory"
              - paragraph [ref=e9483]: Every plausible subfield for Entity type categories turns out to be either behavioral (Observation) or relational (Edge). The category name is constitutive. The depth is already owned by the graph.
              - paragraph [ref=e9484]:
                - strong [ref=e9485]: KNIGHT and FAIRY terminate without depth.
                - text: Same outcome as TECHNICAL on Constraint.
              - separator [ref=e9486]
              - paragraph [ref=e9487]:
                - strong [ref=e9488]: Entity Constitution — Draft
              - group "Code" [ref=e9489]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9491]:
                  - generic [ref=e9492]:
                    - img [ref=e9494]
                    - img [ref=e9498]
                - code [ref=e9502]:
                  - generic [ref=e9503]: "Entity {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // human-readable designator, not personality // Truth: Category type: KNIGHT | FAIRY | ... // extensible — Observatory may expand // no Typed Depth — graph owns behavior }"
              - paragraph [ref=e9504]: That's potentially the entire schema.
              - separator [ref=e9505]
              - paragraph [ref=e9506]:
                - strong [ref=e9507]: What Entity Just Confirmed
              - paragraph [ref=e9508]: "The Observatory Existence Principle applied to schema produces a counterintuitive result:"
              - blockquote [ref=e9509]:
                - paragraph [ref=e9510]: The richer an entity's observable life, the sparser its schema needs to be.
              - paragraph [ref=e9511]:
                - text: Because richness lives in Observations, Discoveries, affinities, and appearances — not in fields. Entity's schema is sparse precisely
                - emphasis [ref=e9512]: because
                - text: its graph presence is dense. The furniture was never lost. It was always somewhere else.
              - separator [ref=e9513]
              - paragraph [ref=e9514]:
                - strong [ref=e9515]: Sector — The Final Node
              - paragraph [ref=e9516]: Sector is likely the cleanest schema in the Observatory. It's a place. Places have names, descriptions, and maybe a type. Everything else — what happened there, what was found there, who is associated with it — is graph territory.
              - paragraph [ref=e9517]:
                - text: "The one question Sector might force: does a place have a"
                - emphasis [ref=e9518]: state
                - text: "? Can a Sector be active, dormant, sealed, discovered? If yes, Sector gets a state field and potentially its first Activation invariant. If no, Sector is just named space and the graph carries everything else."
              - paragraph [ref=e9519]: That question — whether places have lifecycle — is probably the last constitutional decision in Session F. 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e9520]:
            - button "Copy" [ref=e9523]:

            - generic [ref=e9569]:
              - paragraph [ref=e9570]: Locked on Entity. Sector is almost locked — one remaining question to resolve before closing.
              - separator [ref=e9571]
              - paragraph [ref=e9572]:
                - strong [ref=e9573]: The summary Pressure Test
              - paragraph [ref=e9574]: "Apply the Reconstruction Test:"
              - blockquote [ref=e9575]:
                - paragraph [ref=e9576]: Could a future Inspector understand the constitutional purpose of Signal Archive from graph structure alone?
              - paragraph [ref=e9577]: "For Project: no — intent isn't observable. For Tradeoff: no — reasoning isn't observable. For Pattern: no — the specific abstraction isn't observable."
              - paragraph [ref=e9578]: "For Sector: partially."
              - group "Code" [ref=e9579]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9581]:
                  - generic [ref=e9582]:
                    - img [ref=e9584]
                    - img [ref=e9588]
                - code [ref=e9592]:
                  - generic [ref=e9593]: "Signal Archive → OCCURRED_IN ← Observations (type: signal_witnessed)"
                  - text: "Signal Archive → ASSOCIATED_WITH ← Entity: Fairy (affinity: 0.91) Signal Archive → REVEALS ← Discovery: Fairy Origin Located"
              - paragraph [ref=e9594]: A sufficiently dense graph tells an Inspector quite a lot about Signal Archive without a summary. More than it tells them about a Project or a Tradeoff — because places accumulate observable evidence faster than decisions do.
              - paragraph [ref=e9595]:
                - text: But "quite a lot" isn't "everything." The
                - emphasis [ref=e9596]: intent
                - text: of a Sector — why it exists as a named place in the Observatory, what it was designed to hold — may not emerge from evidence alone. Especially for sectors that are new or undiscovered.
              - paragraph [ref=e9597]:
                - strong [ref=e9598]:
                  - text: "Verdict:"
                  - code [ref=e9599]: summary?
                  - text: survives, but optionally.
                - text: A Sector can exist and be meaningful without one — the graph will eventually describe it. But authoring intent deserves a home before the graph has had time to fill in around it.
              - separator [ref=e9600]
              - paragraph [ref=e9601]:
                - strong [ref=e9602]: The SEALED Question — Resolved
              - paragraph [ref=e9603]: "The Accessibility Test is the right filter:"
              - blockquote [ref=e9604]:
                - paragraph [ref=e9605]: Does this condition describe the observer's knowledge, or the place itself?
              - group "Code" [ref=e9606]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9608]:
                  - generic [ref=e9609]:
                    - img [ref=e9611]
                    - img [ref=e9615]
                - code [ref=e9619]:
                  - generic [ref=e9620]: "Observer conditions → rejected:"
                  - text: "Hidden, Discovered, Visible, Known (observer changed, not the Sector) Place conditions → survives: Sealed, Open, Restricted (remains true regardless of who is watching)"
              - paragraph [ref=e9621]: "SEALED specifically: the Sector exists, is known to exist, but cannot be entered. That's a truth claim about the place — not about anyone's awareness of it. It changes what relationships are legal (entities cannot generate OCCURRED_IN observations for a sealed sector) and what traversals are valid."
              - paragraph [ref=e9622]: "That graph consequence is exactly what the Metric Test looks for:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9623]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9625]:
                  - generic [ref=e9626]:
                    - img [ref=e9628]
                    - img [ref=e9632]
                - code [ref=e9636]:
                  - generic [ref=e9637]: "SEALED vs OPEN:"
                  - text: "OCCURRED_IN edges: legal for OPEN, illegal for SEALED Entity affinity: can accumulate for OPEN, frozen for SEALED → graph behavior changes → truth, not presentation"
              - paragraph [ref=e9638]:
                - strong [ref=e9639]: SEALED survives. State field earns its existence.
              - separator [ref=e9640]
              - paragraph [ref=e9641]:
                - strong [ref=e9642]: Sector Constitution — Final
              - group "Code" [ref=e9643]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9645]:
                  - generic [ref=e9646]:

                    - img [ref=e9652]
                - code [ref=e9656]:
                  - generic [ref=e9657]: "Sector {"
                  - text: "// Identity id name // Interpretation summary? // optional — intent that graph can't reconstruct yet // Truth: Category type: ARCHIVE | SIGNAL | ENGINE | RESEARCH | UNKNOWN // Truth: Accessibility State accessibility: OPEN | SEALED | RESTRICTED SEALED { sealedAt: timestamp, sealReason?: string } RESTRICTED { condition: string } // what must be true to enter }"
              - paragraph [ref=e9658]: "New invariant candidate:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9659]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9661]:
                  - generic [ref=e9662]:
                    - img [ref=e9664]
                    - img [ref=e9668]

                  - generic [ref=e9673]: "INV-07: Sector.accessibility = SEALED"
                  - text: → OCCURRED_IN edges targeting this Sector are invalid (Validation Engine enforces — no new observations can be placed inside a sealed sector)
              - separator [ref=e9674]
              - paragraph [ref=e9675]:
                - strong [ref=e9676]: The Observatory Cluster — Complete
              - group "Code" [ref=e9677]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9679]:
                  - generic [ref=e9680]:
                    - img [ref=e9682]
                    - img [ref=e9686]
                - code [ref=e9690]:
                  - generic [ref=e9691]: Entity id, name, type
                  - text: Dense graph presence. Sparse schema. Exists before witnessed. Sector id, name, summary?, type, accessibility Named space with accessibility state. Exists before inhabited.
              - paragraph [ref=e9692]: "Both nodes pass the hard existence test: they survive even when every observation disappears. Their meaning comes from what the graph accumulates around them — not from what their schema declares about them."
              - separator [ref=e9693]
              - paragraph [ref=e9694]:
                - strong [ref=e9695]: Session F — Complete
              - paragraph [ref=e9696]: "All ten node schemas locked:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9697]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9699]:
                  - generic [ref=e9700]:

                    - img [ref=e9706]
