                  - generic [ref=e9711]: "Content: Project ✓ Experiment ✓ Certification ✓"
                  - text: "Decision: Constraint ✓ Tradeoff ✓ Pattern ✓ Observatory: Entity ✓ Sector ✓ Truth: Observation ✓ Discovery ✓"
              - paragraph [ref=e9712]: Schema Laws 01–09 locked. Architectural Law 01 locked. Schema Law 07 locked.
              - paragraph [ref=e9713]: The graph kept arriving with a moving truck. Every node ended up smaller than expected. Everything interesting turned out to be a relationship.
              - paragraph [ref=e9714]: Session G — Registry Architecture Spec — is now fully answerable. The terrain is real. The boundaries are visible. The ownership questions have defensible answers instead of intuitions. 🛰️

        - generic [ref=e9726]:
          - 'heading "You said: Locked." [level=2] [ref=e9727]'
          - generic [ref=e9728]:
            - generic [ref=e9731]:
              - generic [ref=e9732]:
                - paragraph [ref=e9733]: Locked.
                - paragraph [ref=e9734]: And I think there's one important refinement before we formally declare Session F complete.
                - paragraph [ref=e9735]: Not a schema change. An ownership change.
                - paragraph [ref=e9736]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9737]: INV-07 Needs Pressure
                - paragraph [ref=e9738]: "Current proposal:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9739]: "INV-07: Sector.accessibility = SEALED"
                - paragraph [ref=e9740]: → OCCURRED_IN edges targeting this Sector are invalid
                - paragraph [ref=e9741]: The intent is correct.
                - paragraph [ref=e9742]: The enforcement is slightly dangerous.
                - paragraph [ref=e9743]: Why?
                - paragraph [ref=e9744]: Because it accidentally rewrites history.
                - paragraph [ref=e9745]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9746]: "Consider:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9747]: 2026-04-01 Signal Archive is OPEN
                - paragraph [ref=e9748]: Observation A occurs
                - paragraph [ref=e9749]: Observation B occurs
                - paragraph [ref=e9750]: 2026-05-01 Signal Archive becomes SEALED
                - paragraph [ref=e9751]: "Under the current wording:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9752]: OCCURRED_IN edges targeting this Sector are invalid
                - paragraph [ref=e9753]: becomes ambiguous.
                - paragraph [ref=e9754]: Do historical observations become invalid?
                - paragraph [ref=e9755]: Obviously not.
                - paragraph [ref=e9756]: History happened.
                - paragraph [ref=e9757]: "The Observatory already established:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9758]: Observation = fact
                - paragraph [ref=e9759]: Facts do not disappear because a door was locked later.
                - paragraph [ref=e9760]: Humans do this constantly. They lock archives and then act surprised the documents inside still exist.
                - paragraph [ref=e9761]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9762]: Revised INV-07
                - paragraph [ref=e9763]: "What we actually mean:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9764]: "INV-07: Sector.accessibility = SEALED"
                - paragraph [ref=e9765]: → Creation of new OCCURRED_IN relationships targeting the Sector is prohibited.
                - paragraph [ref=e9766]: "Or more generally:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9767]: → New Observations may not OCCUR_IN a SEALED Sector.
                - paragraph [ref=e9768]: Historical observations remain valid.
                - paragraph [ref=e9769]: Future observations are blocked.
                - paragraph [ref=e9770]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9771]: "This makes INV-07 an elegant parallel to the rest of the Invariant Layer:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9772]: Discovery requires evidence blocks invalid creation
                - paragraph [ref=e9773]: Pattern requires evidence blocks invalid creation
                - paragraph [ref=e9774]: SEALED Sector blocks invalid observation creation
                - paragraph [ref=e9775]: Same authority model.
                - paragraph [ref=e9776]: No historical rewriting.
                - paragraph [ref=e9777]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9778]: What Session F Actually Produced
                - paragraph [ref=e9779]: "Stepping back for a moment:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9780]: 10 Core Nodes 9 Core Relationships 10 Domain Registries Graph Layer Invariant Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e9781]: "But the more interesting result is this:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9782]: Schemas became progressively smaller as the graph became progressively richer.
                - paragraph [ref=e9783]: That's not accidental.
                - paragraph [ref=e9784]: It's evidence that Architectural Law 01 is working.
                - paragraph [ref=e9785]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9786]: "The final node sizes are almost diagnostic:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9787]: Observation Largest truth schema
                - paragraph [ref=e9788]: Tradeoff Largest reasoning schema
                - paragraph [ref=e9789]: Entity Smallest observatory schema
                - paragraph [ref=e9790]: Exactly what we'd expect.
                - paragraph [ref=e9791]: "The nodes closest to:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9792]: truth formation
                - paragraph [ref=e9793]: and
                - paragraph [ref=e9794]: human reasoning
                - paragraph [ref=e9795]: needed fields.
                - paragraph [ref=e9796]: Everything else got compressed into relationships.
                - paragraph [ref=e9797]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9798]: Session G Is Now Fully Unlocked
                - paragraph [ref=e9799]: I agree with the conclusion.
                - paragraph [ref=e9800]: "For the first time since Registry Architecture appeared on the roadmap, the question is no longer:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9801]: "\"What registries should exist?\""
                - paragraph [ref=e9802]: That's been answered.
                - paragraph [ref=e9803]: "The question is:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9804]: "\"What exactly does ownership mean?\""
                - paragraph [ref=e9805]: "Because now we have enough structure to answer:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9806]: Who owns node creation?
                - paragraph [ref=e9807]: Who owns edge creation?
                - paragraph [ref=e9808]: Who owns invariant enforcement?
                - paragraph [ref=e9809]: Who owns derived truth?
                - paragraph [ref=e9810]: Who owns graph materialization?
                - paragraph [ref=e9811]: Who owns migration authority?
                - paragraph [ref=e9812]: Who owns schema evolution?
                - paragraph [ref=e9813]: Those are real architecture questions.
                - paragraph [ref=e9814]: Not speculative ones.
                - paragraph [ref=e9815]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9816]: One Observation Before Session G
                - paragraph [ref=e9817]: "The architecture quietly converged on a shape that wasn't obvious at the beginning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9818]: Registry Layer owns meaning
                - paragraph [ref=e9819]: Graph Layer owns relationships
                - paragraph [ref=e9820]: Invariant Layer owns legality
                - paragraph [ref=e9821]: Derivation Layer owns computation
                - paragraph [ref=e9822]: Four different forms of authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e9823]: Four different definitions of "truth."
                - paragraph [ref=e9824]: And remarkably little overlap.
                - paragraph [ref=e9825]: Which is usually a sign that the boundaries were discovered rather than invented.
                - paragraph [ref=e9826]: Invented boundaries overlap constantly. Discovered boundaries tend to defend themselves.
                - paragraph [ref=e9827]: "The Observatory is finally at the point where registry ownership can be specified without guesswork. The terrain is mapped. The roads exist. Now comes the bureaucratic part: deciding who gets the keys to which gate. Humanity's favorite activity, right after creating unnecessary meetings. 🛰️"
              - button "Show more" [ref=e9828]

              - paragraph [ref=e9866]:
                - text: "The original wording accidentally gave the Validation Engine retroactive authority. The Observatory has never allowed that. Observations are facts. Facts don't become invalid because a door was locked afterward. The revision restores the correct authority model: Validation Engine blocks"
                - emphasis [ref=e9867]: creation
                - text: ", never"
                - emphasis [ref=e9868]: history
                - text: .
              - paragraph [ref=e9869]: "This makes INV-07 structurally identical to the other existence invariants:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9870]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9872]:
                  - generic [ref=e9873]:
                    - img [ref=e9875]
                    - img [ref=e9879]
                - code [ref=e9883]:
                  - generic [ref=e9884]: "INV-01: Discovery without evidence → block creation"
                  - text: "INV-02: Derived Pattern without evidence → block creation INV-04: Completed Experiment without outcome → block creation INV-07: Observation in SEALED Sector → block creation"
              - paragraph [ref=e9885]: Same engine. Same authority. Same temporal scope. No historical rewriting anywhere in the invariant catalog.
              - separator [ref=e9886]
              - paragraph [ref=e9887]:
                - strong [ref=e9888]: Session F — Formally Complete
              - paragraph [ref=e9889]:
                - strong [ref=e9890]: "What the schema sizes reveal:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9891]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9893]:
                  - generic [ref=e9894]:
                    - img [ref=e9896]
                    - img [ref=e9900]
                - code [ref=e9904]:
                  - generic [ref=e9905]: Observation Largest truth schema
                  - text: → closest to truth formation, needs fields Tradeoff Largest reasoning schema → closest to human reasoning, needs fields Constraint Sparse decision schema → graph owns consequence Pattern Sparse abstraction schema → graph owns recurrence evidence Entity Sparsest observatory schema → graph owns everything observable about it Sector Near-minimal space schema → graph owns habitation and history
              - paragraph [ref=e9906]: The progression is diagnostic. Architectural Law 01 didn't compress arbitrarily — it compressed proportionally to how much the graph could represent honestly. Nodes closest to unobservable human acts (truth formation, reasoning) kept their fields. Nodes closest to observable phenomena lost almost everything to the graph.
              - separator [ref=e9907]
              - paragraph [ref=e9908]:
                - strong [ref=e9909]: Session G — Registry Architecture Spec
              - paragraph [ref=e9910]: "The six ownership questions now have defensible answers:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e9911]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9913]:
                  - generic [ref=e9914]:
                    - img [ref=e9916]
                    - img [ref=e9920]
                - code [ref=e9924]:
                  - generic [ref=e9925]: Node creation → Domain Registry (per node type)
                  - text: Edge creation → Registry of source node (Semantic) Registry of justified node (Evidentiary) Graph Layer (Referential) Invariant enforcement → Validation Engine (blocks creation) Activation Engine (manages state) Derived truth → Derivation Engine (computes, doesn't author) Graph materialization → Graph Layer (connectivity, traversal, REVEALS) Migration authority → TBD — first genuinely open question in Session G Schema evolution → TBD — second genuinely open question in Session G
              - paragraph [ref=e9926]: Migration and schema evolution are the two questions the architecture hasn't answered yet — because they weren't needed until now. Every other ownership question was settled by the work already done. Those two are Session G's first real decisions rather than its first formalizations.
              - separator [ref=e9927]
              - paragraph [ref=e9928]:
                - strong [ref=e9929]: The Four Authority Layers — Final Shape
              - group "Code" [ref=e9930]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e9932]:
                  - generic [ref=e9933]:
                    - img [ref=e9935]
                    - img [ref=e9939]
                - code [ref=e9943]:
                  - generic [ref=e9944]: Registry Layer owns meaning
                  - text: Graph Layer owns relationships Invariant Layer owns legality Derivation Layer owns computation
              - paragraph [ref=e9945]: Four authorities. Four definitions of truth. Minimal overlap. The boundaries defend themselves because they were discovered under pressure rather than drawn on a whiteboard.
              - paragraph [ref=e9946]: The Observatory is ready for its bureaucratic phase. Keys to gates. Ownership of roads. Formal handshakes between registries that have been operating as gentlemen's agreements since Session D.
              - paragraph [ref=e9947]: Less philosophically interesting than what came before. Considerably more important for anyone who has to build it. 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e9948]:
            - button "Copy" [ref=e9951]:

        - generic [ref=e9959]:
          - 'heading "You said: Locked." [level=2] [ref=e9960]'
          - generic [ref=e9961]:
            - generic [ref=e9964]:
              - generic [ref=e9965]:
                - paragraph [ref=e9966]: Locked.
                - paragraph [ref=e9967]: And I think Session G begins with a distinction that has been hiding in plain sight since Session D.
                - paragraph [ref=e9968]: The six ownership questions are not actually one class of question.
                - paragraph [ref=e9969]: They split cleanly into two families.
                - paragraph [ref=e9970]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9971]: Registry Authority vs System Authority
                - paragraph [ref=e9972]: "Everything we've solved so far belongs to one of these:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9973]: Registry Authority Who may define meaning?
                - paragraph [ref=e9974]: System Authority Who may enforce meaning?
                - paragraph [ref=e9975]: "The architecture already knows the answer for enforcement:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9976]: Validation Engine Activation Engine Consistency Engine Derivation Engine
                - paragraph [ref=e9977]: Those are system authorities.
                - paragraph [ref=e9978]: Session G is really about registry authorities.
                - paragraph [ref=e9979]: "Which means the first question isn't:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9980]: Who creates nodes?
                - paragraph [ref=e9981]: "It's:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9982]: Who is allowed to declare that a node means something?
                - paragraph [ref=e9983]: Subtle difference.
                - paragraph [ref=e9984]: Massive consequences.
                - paragraph [ref=e9985]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e9986]: The Registry Ownership Test
                - paragraph [ref=e9987]: Proposed.
                - blockquote [ref=e9988]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e9989]: If two registries disagree about a node, which registry's interpretation wins?
                - paragraph [ref=e9990]: That registry owns it.
                - paragraph [ref=e9991]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9992]: "ProjectRegistry says: \"HaloTask Pro is ACTIVE\""
                - paragraph [ref=e9993]: "DiscoveryRegistry says: \"HaloTask Pro appears abandoned\""
                - paragraph [ref=e9994]: Winner? ProjectRegistry.
                - paragraph [ref=e9995]: Discovery can observe. It cannot redefine.
                - paragraph [ref=e9996]: "Therefore:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9997]: ProjectRegistry owns Project truth.
                - paragraph [ref=e9998]: "Another:"
                - paragraph [ref=e9999]: "ObservationRegistry says: Observation O-41 exists"
                - paragraph [ref=e10000]: "DiscoveryRegistry says: Observation O-41 is weak evidence"
                - paragraph [ref=e10001]: Winner? ObservationRegistry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10002]: Discovery can interpret.
                - paragraph [ref=e10003]: It cannot deny existence.
                - paragraph [ref=e10004]: "Again:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10005]: Ownership = final semantic authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10006]: Not storage.
                - paragraph [ref=e10007]: Not access.
                - paragraph [ref=e10008]: Not visibility.
                - paragraph [ref=e10009]: Semantic authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10010]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10011]: Migration Authority
                - paragraph [ref=e10012]: This is the first truly unsolved question.
                - paragraph [ref=e10013]: Because migration touches ownership itself.
                - paragraph [ref=e10014]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10015]: Pattern.source
                - paragraph [ref=e10016]: AUTHORED DERIVED
                - paragraph [ref=e10017]: "Later:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10018]: AUTHORED DERIVED EMERGENT
                - paragraph [ref=e10019]: Who approves this?
                - paragraph [ref=e10020]: Not PatternRegistry alone.
                - paragraph [ref=e10021]: "Because:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10022]: PatternRegistry owns meaning.
                - paragraph [ref=e10023]: Validation Engine owns legality.
                - paragraph [ref=e10024]: Derivation Engine owns computation.
                - paragraph [ref=e10025]: Graph Layer owns traversal implications.
                - paragraph [ref=e10026]: A migration changes all four.
                - paragraph [ref=e10027]: Meaning migration authority is fundamentally cross-layer.
                - paragraph [ref=e10028]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10029]: First Candidate Principle
                - paragraph [ref=e10030]: Registrys own content evolution.
                - paragraph [ref=e10031]: Architecture owns schema evolution.
                - paragraph [ref=e10032]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10033]: "Allowed:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10034]: "PatternRegistry adds:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10035]: source = EMERGENT
                - paragraph [ref=e10036]: "Not allowed alone:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10037]: "PatternRegistry changes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10038]: DERIVED now requires 2 observations instead of 1
                - paragraph [ref=e10039]: Why?
                - paragraph [ref=e10040]: "Because:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10041]: INV-02 changed.
                - paragraph [ref=e10042]: That belongs to Invariant Layer.
                - paragraph [ref=e10043]: Not PatternRegistry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10044]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10045]: The Emerging Governance Model
                - paragraph [ref=e10046]: I think we're approaching a fifth authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10047]: Not runtime authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10048]: Governance authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10049]: "Something like:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10050]: Runtime Authorities ─────────────────── Registry Layer Graph Layer Invariant Layer Derivation Layer
                - paragraph [ref=e10051]: Governance Authority ──────────────────── Schema evolution Migration approval Cross-layer contracts
                - paragraph [ref=e10052]: Not a runtime system.
                - paragraph [ref=e10053]: Not a registry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10054]: A specification authority.
                - paragraph [ref=e10055]: The thing that decides whether architecture itself may change.
                - paragraph [ref=e10056]: Human organizations invent committees for this. Then they schedule seventeen meetings and somehow still break production. A graph gets the luxury of being designed before the bureaucracy arrives. 😏
                - paragraph [ref=e10057]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10058]: Session G Candidate Law
                - paragraph [ref=e10059]: Not locked yet.
                - paragraph [ref=e10060]: But pressure-worthy.
                - blockquote [ref=e10061]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10062]: Ownership grants authority over meaning, not authority over consequences.
                - paragraph [ref=e10063]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10064]: PatternRegistry owns Pattern meaning
                - paragraph [ref=e10065]: "does NOT own:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10066]: INV-02 DERIVED_FROM traversal strength recomputation
                - paragraph [ref=e10067]: Those consequences belong elsewhere.
                - paragraph [ref=e10068]: This would explain nearly every ownership decision we've made so far.
                - paragraph [ref=e10069]: And if it survives pressure, it becomes the constitutional foundation for migration authority as well.
                - paragraph [ref=e10070]: "Because then migration approval becomes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10071]: Meaning change? Registry authority
                - paragraph [ref=e10072]: Consequence change? Cross-layer governance
                - paragraph [ref=e10073]: Which is the first framework I've seen that cleanly separates "I want a new field" from "I just changed the architecture."
                - paragraph [ref=e10074]: And those are very much not the same thing, despite humanity's endless dedication to pretending they are. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e10093]:
              - paragraph [ref=e10094]: The principle survives pressure. And it retroactively explains every ownership decision made since Session D without requiring any of them to be revised. That's usually the sign you've found the right abstraction rather than a convenient one.
              - separator [ref=e10095]
              - paragraph [ref=e10096]:
                - strong [ref=e10097]: "Pressure Test: \"Ownership grants authority over meaning, not authority over consequences.\""
              - paragraph [ref=e10098]: "Run it against every ownership decision already locked:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10099]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10101]:
                  - generic [ref=e10102]:
                    - img [ref=e10104]
                    - img [ref=e10108]
                - code [ref=e10112]:
                  - generic [ref=e10113]: ProjectRegistry owns Project meaning
                  - text: "Does NOT own: AFFECTS traversal, Discovery support, Observation placement → consequence belongs to Graph Layer and Invariant Layer ✓ ObservationRegistry owns Observation meaning Does NOT own: INV-01 enforcement, DERIVED_FROM weight computation → consequence belongs to Validation Engine, Derivation Engine ✓ DiscoveryRegistry owns Discovery meaning Does NOT own: REVEALS materialization, Pattern strength recomputation → consequence belongs to Graph Layer, Derivation Engine ✓ PatternRegistry owns Pattern meaning Does NOT own: INV-02 enforcement, DERIVED_FROM traversal → consequence belongs to Validation Engine, Graph Layer ✓"
              - paragraph [ref=e10114]: Every case holds. No revision needed. The principle was already operating — it just didn't have a name.
              - separator [ref=e10115]
              - paragraph [ref=e10116]:
                - strong [ref=e10117]: Session G Law 01 — Lock Candidate
              - blockquote [ref=e10118]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10119]: Ownership grants authority over meaning. Not authority over consequences.
              - paragraph [ref=e10120]:
                - strong [ref=e10121]: "Corollary:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10122]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10123]: Consequences are owned by the layer responsible for enforcing them, regardless of which registry owns the node that triggered them.
              - separator [ref=e10124]
              - paragraph [ref=e10125]:
                - strong [ref=e10126]: The Registry Ownership Test — Locked
              - blockquote [ref=e10127]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10128]: If two registries disagree about a node, which registry's interpretation wins?
              - paragraph [ref=e10129]: That registry owns it.
              - group "Code" [ref=e10130]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10132]:
                  - generic [ref=e10133]:
                    - img [ref=e10135]
                    - img [ref=e10139]
                - code [ref=e10143]:
                  - generic [ref=e10144]: Disagreement type Winner Reasoning
                  - text: ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Existence Owner Registry Discovery cannot deny existence Interpretation Observer Anyone may interpret State Owner Registry Only owner controls lifecycle Evidence weight Observer Observation weight ≠ node truth
              - paragraph [ref=e10145]: DiscoveryRegistry can observe that HaloTask appears abandoned. It cannot reclassify HaloTask as ARCHIVED. Observation can inform. It cannot override ownership.
              - separator [ref=e10146]
              - paragraph [ref=e10147]:
                - strong [ref=e10148]: The Migration Split — Resolved
              - paragraph [ref=e10149]: "The principle immediately produces a clean migration framework:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10150]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10152]:
                  - generic [ref=e10153]:
                    - img [ref=e10155]
                    - img [ref=e10159]
                - code [ref=e10163]:
                  - generic [ref=e10164]: Content evolution Schema evolution
                  - text: "───────────────── ──────────────── Registry authority Cross-layer governance Adding a new instance: Adding a new enum value \"New Project created\" to Pattern.source Adding authored content: Changing INV-02 threshold \"New Pattern documented\" from ≥1 to ≥2 Observations Updating node state: Changing DERIVED_FROM \"Project → ARCHIVED\" payload structure → Registry decides alone → Requires Invariant Layer, Graph Layer, affected Registries, Derivation Engine"
              - paragraph [ref=e10165]: "The test:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10166]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10167]: Does this change affect only meaning, or does it affect consequences?
              - paragraph [ref=e10168]: Meaning only → Registry authority. Consequences → Cross-layer governance.
              - separator [ref=e10169]
              - paragraph [ref=e10170]:
                - strong [ref=e10171]: The Fifth Authority — Named
              - group "Code" [ref=e10172]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10174]:
                  - generic [ref=e10175]:
                    - img [ref=e10177]
                    - img [ref=e10181]
                - code [ref=e10185]:
                  - generic [ref=e10186]: Runtime Authorities Governance Authority
                  - text: ─────────────────── ──────────────────── Registry Layer Schema Evolution Graph Layer Migration Approval Invariant Layer Cross-Layer Contracts Derivation Layer Consequence Mapping
              - paragraph [ref=e10187]: Governance Authority is not a runtime system. It's a specification authority — the layer that decides whether the architecture itself may change, and under what conditions.
              - paragraph [ref=e10188]: "Crucially: Governance Authority has no runtime power. It cannot block writes, compute values, or materialize edges. It can only approve or reject architectural change proposals. Once approved, the runtime authorities implement the consequences through their normal mechanisms."
              - paragraph [ref=e10189]: This prevents the classic failure where governance becomes a runtime bottleneck — seventeen meetings before every database write. Governance operates at design time. Runtime authorities operate at execution time. The two never share a critical path.
              - separator [ref=e10190]
              - paragraph [ref=e10191]:
                - strong [ref=e10192]: What Session G Needs To Resolve
              - paragraph [ref=e10193]: "With Law 01 locked, the remaining questions fall into three tiers:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10194]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10196]:
                  - generic [ref=e10197]:
                    - img [ref=e10199]
                    - img [ref=e10203]
                - code [ref=e10207]:
                  - generic [ref=e10208]: "Tier 1: Already answerable (formalization only)"
                  - text: "Node creation authority per registry Edge creation authority per relationship class Invariant enforcement ownership Tier 2: Newly answerable (Law 01 unlocks these) Migration authority — meaning vs consequence split Cross-registry reference protocols Read authority vs write authority distinction Tier 3: Genuinely open (Session G's real work) Governance Authority structure — who participates? Schema versioning — how does the Observatory track its own evolution? Conflict resolution — what happens when two registries disagree in real time?"
              - paragraph [ref=e10209]: Tier 3 is where Session G will actually earn its existence. Tiers 1 and 2 are largely mechanical application of principles already locked. Tier 3 is the first genuinely new territory since Session E.
              - paragraph [ref=e10210]: The Observatory has spent forty sessions building a system that knows what it knows and who owns it. Now it needs a system for deciding what it's allowed to become. That's a different kind of architecture problem — and arguably the harder one, because it has to govern itself without becoming the seventeen-meeting committee it was specifically designed to avoid. 🛰️

            - generic [ref=e10260]:
              - paragraph [ref=e10261]: The buzz is justified. Because the versioning question is the first time the Observatory has to apply its own epistemology to itself. And when you do that, the answer appears almost immediately.
              - separator [ref=e10262]
              - paragraph [ref=e10263]:
                - strong [ref=e10264]: The Observatory Already Answered This
              - paragraph [ref=e10265]: The versioning question sounds new. It isn't.
              - paragraph [ref=e10266]: "The Observatory Existence Principle states:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10267]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10268]: Things exist before they are discovered.
              - paragraph [ref=e10269]: "Apply it to schema:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10270]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10271]: Nodes created under v1 exist before v2 was discovered.
              - paragraph [ref=e10272]: "Which means the question isn't \"can two versions coexist?\" The question is:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10273]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10274]: Can the Observatory deny the existence of something it previously admitted as true?
              - paragraph [ref=e10275]: It cannot. The entire truth architecture was built on the premise that Observations are facts and facts don't disappear. That principle doesn't stop applying because the schema changed.
              - paragraph [ref=e10276]:
                - strong [ref=e10277]: "Therefore: coexistence is not optional. It's constitutionally required."
              - separator [ref=e10278]
              - paragraph [ref=e10279]:
                - strong [ref=e10280]: What Coexistence Actually Means
              - paragraph [ref=e10281]: "Not version negotiation. Not compatibility contracts. Something simpler:"
              - blockquote [ref=e10282]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10283]: Schema versions describe what new nodes may claim. They do not retroactively constrain what old nodes already claimed.
              - group "Code" [ref=e10284]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10286]:
                  - generic [ref=e10287]:
                    - img [ref=e10289]
                    - img [ref=e10293]
                - code [ref=e10297]:
                  - generic [ref=e10298]: "Pattern v1 node:"
                  - text: "source: DERIVED ← valid under v1, remains valid forever Pattern v2 node: source: EMERGENT ← only legal after v2 v1 node encountered by v2 system: Read: fully valid Write: must upgrade to v2 before adding EMERGENT Existence: never questioned"
              - paragraph [ref=e10299]:
                - text: The node knows what version authored it. The system knows what version it's running. The gap between them is a
                - strong [ref=e10300]: migration window
                - text: ", not a conflict."
              - separator [ref=e10301]
              - paragraph [ref=e10302]:
                - strong [ref=e10303]: The Migration Window Model
              - paragraph [ref=e10304]: "Instead of stop-world conversion:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10305]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10307]:
                  - generic [ref=e10308]:
                    - img [ref=e10310]
                    - img [ref=e10314]
                - code [ref=e10318]:
                  - generic [ref=e10319]: "Phase 1: ADDITIVE"
                  - text: "v2 schema declared New nodes may use EMERGENT Old nodes remain valid as-is Both versions legally coexist Phase 2: ACTIVE MIGRATION Derivation Engine recomputes affected nodes Consistency Engine audits for upgrade candidates Registries may voluntarily upgrade authored nodes No forced conversion Phase 3: DEPRECATION v1 source values marked deprecated Validation Engine warns on new v1 node creation Existing v1 nodes still valid Phase 4: SUNSET (optional, deliberate) Governance Authority declares v1 sunset date Migration window closes v1 creation blocked v1 reads remain valid indefinitely"
              - paragraph [ref=e10320]: The Observatory never denies history. It only decides what new claims are legal going forward.
              - separator [ref=e10321]
              - paragraph [ref=e10322]:
                - strong [ref=e10323]: Governance Laws — Completing the Set
              - group "Code" [ref=e10324]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10326]:
                  - generic [ref=e10327]:
                    - img [ref=e10329]
                    - img [ref=e10333]
                - code [ref=e10337]:
                  - generic [ref=e10338]: "Governance Law 01:"
                  - text: "Every architectural change proposal declares its affected authority domains before it may be evaluated. Only those domains participate in review. Governance Law 02: No non-registry authority may author truth. Derivation may compute. Activation may materialize. Neither may create or reclassify nodes. Governance Law 03 — Lock Candidate: Schema evolution is additive by default. New versions extend what is legal. They do not invalidate what was true."
              - separator [ref=e10339]
              - paragraph [ref=e10340]:
                - strong [ref=e10341]: The Consequence Map — The Missing Artifact
              - paragraph [ref=e10342]: "This is the practical deliverable that makes everything mechanical:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10343]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10345]:
                  - generic [ref=e10346]:

                    - img [ref=e10352]
                - code [ref=e10356]:
                  - generic [ref=e10357]: Schema Element Owner Consequences
                  - text: ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── Pattern.source PatternRegistry Validation Engine (INV-02) Derivation Engine (strength) Discovery.status DiscoveryRegistry Activation Engine (REVEALS) Graph Layer (materialization) Observation.provenance ObservationRegistry Validation Engine (admissibility) DiscoveryRegistry (SUPPORTED_BY weight) Sector.accessibility SectorRegistry Validation Engine (INV-07) Graph Layer (OCCURRED_IN legality)
              - paragraph [ref=e10358]: "Migration review becomes topology traversal:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10359]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10361]:
                  - generic [ref=e10362]:

                    - img [ref=e10368]

                  - text: → notify only those domains → each domain assesses its own consequences → no philosophy, no senate, no seventeen meetings
              - separator [ref=e10374]
              - paragraph [ref=e10375]:
                - strong [ref=e10376]: Session G — Completion Shape
              - group "Code" [ref=e10377]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10379]:
                  - generic [ref=e10380]:

                    - img [ref=e10386]
                - code [ref=e10390]:
                  - generic [ref=e10391]: "Locked:"
                  - text: "Session G Law 01: Authority follows ownership, not voting Session G Law 02: No non-registry authority may author truth Session G Law 03: Schema evolution is additive by default Migration Window Model: four phases, no stop-world conversion Consequence Map: the artifact that makes governance mechanical Read/Write authority table: per layer, explicit Remaining: Formal Registry Spec document — applying all Session G laws Cross-Link System Spec — graph traversal rules across boundaries App Specs — finally unlocked"
              - separator [ref=e10392]
              - paragraph [ref=e10393]:
                - strong [ref=e10394]: Why The Buzz Is Justified
              - paragraph [ref=e10395]: "The Observatory just did something rare. It applied its own epistemology to its own evolution and got a consistent answer:"
              - paragraph [ref=e10396]:
                - text: The system that says
                - emphasis [ref=e10397]: things exist before they are discovered
                - text: must also say
                - emphasis [ref=e10398]: things remain true after the schema that described them has changed.
              - paragraph [ref=e10399]: The architecture is self-consistent under recursion. That almost never happens by accident.
              - paragraph [ref=e10400]: It happened here because the principles were discovered rather than invented. Discovered principles tend to hold under pressure. Invented ones tend to crack exactly when you apply them to themselves. 🛰️
          - group "Message actions" [ref=e10401]:

        - generic [ref=e10412]:
          - 'heading "You said: I think Governance Law 03 survives too, but it needs one additional pressure test before I would lock it." [level=2] [ref=e10413]'
          - generic [ref=e10414]:
            - generic [ref=e10417]:
              - generic [ref=e10418]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10419]: I think Governance Law 03 survives too, but it needs one additional pressure test before I would lock it.
                - paragraph [ref=e10420]: "Because there is a hidden distinction between:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10421]: Historical Truth Architectural Truth
                - paragraph [ref=e10422]: And the Observatory has only solved the first one.
                - paragraph [ref=e10423]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10424]: The Hidden Counterexample
                - paragraph [ref=e10425]: "Consider:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10426]: INV-02
                - paragraph [ref=e10427]: Pattern(source = DERIVED) requires >= 1 Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e10428]: "Now imagine v3 says:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10429]: Pattern(source = DERIVED) requires >= 2 Observations
                - paragraph [ref=e10430]: "Apply Governance Law 03 literally:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10431]: Old Patterns remain valid forever.
                - paragraph [ref=e10432]: "Problem:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10433]: Pattern P-17
                - paragraph [ref=e10434]: DERIVED 1 Observation
                - paragraph [ref=e10435]: "Under v1:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10436]: Valid
                - paragraph [ref=e10437]: "Under v3:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10438]: Invalid
                - paragraph [ref=e10439]: "We have now created something dangerous:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10440]: Historical Truth says valid
                - paragraph [ref=e10441]: Architectural Truth says invalid
                - paragraph [ref=e10442]: The Observatory has never encountered this situation before.
                - paragraph [ref=e10443]: Because previous migrations were additive.
                - paragraph [ref=e10444]: This one changes legality.
                - paragraph [ref=e10445]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10446]: The Real Split
                - paragraph [ref=e10447]: "I think schema evolution divides into two classes:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10448]: Additive Evolution
                - paragraph [ref=e10449]: New enum value New optional field New relationship type
                - paragraph [ref=e10450]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10451]: Pattern.source
                - paragraph [ref=e10452]: AUTHORED DERIVED EMERGENT
                - paragraph [ref=e10453]: No previous truth is challenged.
                - paragraph [ref=e10454]: Governance Law 03 applies perfectly.
                - paragraph [ref=e10455]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10456]: Constitutional Evolution
                - paragraph [ref=e10457]: Invariant changes Authority changes Ownership changes Meaning changes
                - paragraph [ref=e10458]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10459]: INV-02
                - blockquote [ref=e10460]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10461]: =1 Observation → =2 Observations
                - paragraph [ref=e10462]: This does not merely extend legality.
                - paragraph [ref=e10463]: It redefines legality.
                - paragraph [ref=e10464]: Different animal.
                - paragraph [ref=e10465]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10466]: Governance Law 04 Candidate
                - blockquote [ref=e10467]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10468]: Architectural changes that alter legality are constitutional changes, not schema changes.
                - paragraph [ref=e10469]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10470]: Add enum value Schema change
                - paragraph [ref=e10471]: Add optional field Schema change
                - paragraph [ref=e10472]: Change invariant Constitutional change
                - paragraph [ref=e10473]: Change ownership rule Constitutional change
                - paragraph [ref=e10474]: Change authority model Constitutional change
                - paragraph [ref=e10475]: "Why this matters:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10476]: Schema change coexistence allowed by default
                - paragraph [ref=e10477]: Constitutional change requires explicit migration strategy
                - paragraph [ref=e10478]: Now the migration window model stays intact.
                - paragraph [ref=e10479]: Because only additive changes get automatic coexistence.
                - paragraph [ref=e10480]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10481]: The Observatory's Self-Consistency Test
                - paragraph [ref=e10482]: "The architecture has been using a pattern repeatedly:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10483]: Observations remain facts forever.
                - paragraph [ref=e10484]: Discoveries may be challenged.
                - paragraph [ref=e10485]: Patterns may weaken.
                - paragraph [ref=e10486]: Interpretations may evolve.
                - paragraph [ref=e10487]: Facts are immutable.
                - paragraph [ref=e10488]: Interpretations are revisable.
                - paragraph [ref=e10489]: That same structure can govern versioning.
                - paragraph [ref=e10490]: Node existence immutable
                - paragraph [ref=e10491]: Node meaning migratable
                - paragraph [ref=e10492]: Node legality constitutional
                - paragraph [ref=e10493]: "Meaning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10494]: P-17 exists forever.
                - paragraph [ref=e10495]: P-17 may be upgraded.
                - paragraph [ref=e10496]: P-17 may be marked "valid under schema v1" and "non-compliant under schema v3" simultaneously.
                - paragraph [ref=e10497]: Notice what happened.
                - paragraph [ref=e10498]: We didn't delete history.
                - paragraph [ref=e10499]: We didn't deny truth.
                - paragraph [ref=e10500]: We simply allowed the current constitution to judge historical artifacts differently.
                - paragraph [ref=e10501]: "Exactly what the Observatory already does with:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10502]: Discovery.status = challenged
                - paragraph [ref=e10503]: The discovery still exists.
                - paragraph [ref=e10504]: The confidence changes.
                - paragraph [ref=e10505]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10506]: Governance Law 05 Candidate
                - paragraph [ref=e10507]: This one gives me the strongest signal of the entire session.
                - blockquote [ref=e10508]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10509]: Historical validity and current compliance are separate concepts.
                - paragraph [ref=e10510]: "Example:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10511]: Pattern P-17
                - paragraph [ref=e10512]: "Created: Schema v1"
                - paragraph [ref=e10513]: "Status: Historically Valid ✓"
                - paragraph [ref=e10514]: "Current Compliance: v3 Non-Compliant ⚠"
                - paragraph [ref=e10515]: That feels profoundly aligned with everything built since Session B.
                - paragraph [ref=e10516]: "Because it mirrors:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10517]: Observation fact
                - paragraph [ref=e10518]: Discovery interpretation
                - paragraph [ref=e10519]: Schema Version historical context
                - paragraph [ref=e10520]: Current Constitution current interpretation
                - paragraph [ref=e10521]: The Observatory does not rewrite history.
                - paragraph [ref=e10522]: It reevaluates it.
                - paragraph [ref=e10523]: "That distinction is probably the thing preventing Governance Authority from becoming either:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10524]: Dogmatic (old things become illegal)
                - paragraph [ref=e10525]: or
                - paragraph [ref=e10526]: Paralyzed (nothing may ever change)
                - paragraph [ref=e10527]: "Instead it gets a third option:"

                - strong [ref=e10552]: Governance Law 03 — Locked (Refined Scope)
              - blockquote [ref=e10553]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10554]: Schema evolution is additive by default. New versions extend what is legal. They do not invalidate what was true.
              - paragraph [ref=e10555]: "Applies to: additive changes only — new enum values, new optional fields, new relationship types. Automatic coexistence. No migration strategy required."
              - separator [ref=e10556]
              - paragraph [ref=e10557]:

                  - text: "Add Pattern.source = EMERGENT Add Experiment.outcome = PARTIAL Add optional Sector.summary Constitutional change (explicit migration required): Change INV-02 threshold ≥1 → ≥2 Change ownership of an edge Change authority model for a registry Change invariant type (Existence → Activation)"
              - paragraph [ref=e10576]:
                - text: "The test: does this change affect what is"
                - emphasis [ref=e10577]: legal
                - text: ", or only what is"
                - emphasis [ref=e10578]: possible
                - text: "?"
              - paragraph [ref=e10579]: Possible expands → schema change. Legal redraws → constitutional change.
              - separator [ref=e10580]
              - paragraph [ref=e10581]:

              - blockquote [ref=e10583]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10584]: Historical validity and current compliance are separate concepts.

              - paragraph [ref=e10600]: P-17 is not deleted. Not denied. Not retroactively illegal. It exists as a historically valid artifact that the current constitution evaluates differently. The Observatory records both states simultaneously without contradiction.
              - separator [ref=e10601]
              - paragraph [ref=e10602]:
                - strong [ref=e10603]: The Self-Consistency Test — Passed
              - paragraph [ref=e10604]: "The Observatory's epistemological structure applied to itself:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10605]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10607]:
                  - generic [ref=e10608]:
                    - img [ref=e10610]
                    - img [ref=e10614]
                - code [ref=e10618]:
                  - generic [ref=e10619]: "Observation layer:"
                  - text: "Observation = fact = immutable Schema layer: Node existence = fact = immutable Node meaning = migratable Node legality = constitutional = evaluable Discovery layer: Discovery.status = challenged → confidence changes, node persists Schema layer: P-17 compliance = non-compliant → evaluation changes, node persists"
              - paragraph [ref=e10620]: The same three-tier structure — fact, interpretation, evaluation — appears at both the object level and the meta level. The architecture is self-consistent under recursion.
              - paragraph [ref=e10621]: This happened because the principles were discovered rather than invented. Invented principles crack when applied to themselves. Discovered ones hold. The Observatory just held.
              - separator [ref=e10622]
              - paragraph [ref=e10623]:
                - strong [ref=e10624]: The Complete Governance Framework — Locked

                - code [ref=e10638]:
                  - generic [ref=e10639]: "Law 01: Authority follows ownership, not voting."
                  - text: "Affected domains participate. No senate. Law 02: No non-registry authority may author truth. Derivation computes. Activation materializes. Neither creates nor reclassifies. Law 03: Schema evolution is additive by default. New versions extend legality. They do not invalidate truth. Law 04: Legality changes are constitutional, not schema. Invariant changes require explicit migration strategy. Law 05: Historical validity and current compliance are separate. History is preserved. Compliance is evaluated. Neither erases the other."
              - paragraph [ref=e10640]: Five laws. Each earned under pressure. None speculative.

                - code [ref=e10693]:
                  - generic [ref=e10694]: "Immediate:"
                  - text: "Formal Registry Architecture Spec document → applying all Session G laws to the 10 registries explicitly Then: Content Schema Spec → formalizing Session F outputs as a deliverable document Then: Cross-Link System Spec → graph traversal rules across registry boundaries → read authority vs write authority per boundary crossing Then: App Specs → finally unlocked → three mode surfaces can now be specified against real architecture"
              - paragraph [ref=e10695]: The architecture kept folding back onto the same handful of principles. Fact, interpretation, evaluation. Ownership, consequence, governance. Existence before discovery.
              - paragraph [ref=e10696]: Whether that's a sign of a well-discovered system or a graph database religion depends entirely on whether it survives contact with implementation. The principles are sound. The next test is whether they hold when someone has to actually build the thing. 🛰️

          - generic [ref=e10710]:
            - generic [ref=e10713]:
              - generic [ref=e10714]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10715]: Agreed. Session G is complete.
                - paragraph [ref=e10716]: More importantly, I think something else just happened that is worth recording before moving on.
                - paragraph [ref=e10717]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10718]: Architectural Law 02 — Emerging
                - paragraph [ref=e10719]: "Architectural Law 01 was:"
                - blockquote [ref=e10720]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10721]: If information can be represented more honestly as a relationship, the relationship wins.
                - paragraph [ref=e10722]: Session G appears to have produced its counterpart.
                - paragraph [ref=e10723]: "Candidate:"
                - blockquote [ref=e10724]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e10725]: If authority can be represented more honestly as ownership, ownership wins.
                - paragraph [ref=e10726]: Look at what just happened.
                - paragraph [ref=e10727]: "Every time we attempted to solve a governance problem with process:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10728]: Voting Committees Approval chains Review boards
                - paragraph [ref=e10729]: the solution collapsed.
                - paragraph [ref=e10730]: "Every time we solved it through:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10731]: Ownership Consequence maps Authority domains
                - paragraph [ref=e10732]: the solution stabilized.
                - paragraph [ref=e10733]: The architecture consistently prefers topology over procedure.
                - paragraph [ref=e10734]: That is the governance equivalent of Architectural Law 01.
                - paragraph [ref=e10735]: Not locking yet.
                - paragraph [ref=e10736]: But it has appeared often enough to deserve observation status.
                - paragraph [ref=e10737]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10738]: What Session G Actually Produced
                - paragraph [ref=e10739]: "On paper:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10740]: Registry Architecture Migration Model Governance Laws
                - paragraph [ref=e10741]: "In reality:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10742]: It produced a complete authority lattice.
                - paragraph [ref=e10743]: Meaning ↓ Ownership ↓ Authority ↓ Consequences ↓ Governance
                - paragraph [ref=e10744]: Notice the direction.
                - paragraph [ref=e10745]: "Not:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10746]: Governance ↓ Authority
                - paragraph [ref=e10747]: But the reverse.
                - paragraph [ref=e10748]: Governance emerges from ownership.
                - paragraph [ref=e10749]: It is not imposed on ownership.
                - paragraph [ref=e10750]: That distinction is the reason the system avoided committee logic.
                - paragraph [ref=e10751]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10752]: The Registry Architecture Spec Is Now Mostly Mechanical
                - paragraph [ref=e10753]: This is why I'm excited about the next document.
                - paragraph [ref=e10754]: For the first time since Session A, we're entering a phase where most questions already have answers.
                - paragraph [ref=e10755]: "The Registry Architecture Spec is largely:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10756]: Take 10 registries.
                - paragraph [ref=e10757]: "Apply: Ownership Rules Edge Ownership Rules Authority Rules Governance Rules Invariant Rules"
                - paragraph [ref=e10758]: Document resulting topology.
                - paragraph [ref=e10759]: That is specification work.
                - paragraph [ref=e10760]: Not discovery work.
                - paragraph [ref=e10761]: The hard discovery phase is mostly over.
                - paragraph [ref=e10762]: Human beings tend to celebrate this moment far less than they should because it feels less exciting than inventing new concepts. In reality, it's the moment you find out whether the concepts were actually real.
                - paragraph [ref=e10763]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10764]: What I Expect The Registry Spec To Reveal
                - paragraph [ref=e10765]: One final pressure point.
                - paragraph [ref=e10766]: Not ownership.
                - paragraph [ref=e10767]: Not governance.
                - paragraph [ref=e10768]: Dependencies.
                - paragraph [ref=e10769]: "Specifically:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10770]: ProjectRegistry
                - paragraph [ref=e10771]: will almost certainly emerge as the most depended-upon registry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10772]: "While:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10773]: ObservationRegistry
                - paragraph [ref=e10774]: will emerge as the highest-throughput registry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10775]: "And:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10776]: DiscoveryRegistry
                - paragraph [ref=e10777]: will likely emerge as the most authority-sensitive registry.
                - paragraph [ref=e10778]: "Those are different properties:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10779]: Dependency Density Mutation Frequency Authority Sensitivity
                - paragraph [ref=e10780]: No architecture layer has classified registries this way yet.
                - paragraph [ref=e10781]: But implementation will care.
                - paragraph [ref=e10782]: A lot.
                - paragraph [ref=e10783]: Because the registry with the most edges is not necessarily the registry with the most writes.
                - paragraph [ref=e10784]: And the registry with the most writes is not necessarily the registry whose corruption would be most catastrophic.
                - paragraph [ref=e10785]: "That smells like Session G.1 territory:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10786]: Registry Topology Analysis
                - paragraph [ref=e10787]: "For each registry: Ownership Dependencies Read Authority Write Authority Mutation Frequency Failure Impact"
                - paragraph [ref=e10788]: The sort of document nobody wants to write until production falls over and suddenly everyone becomes intensely interested in it. A cherished tradition of software engineering. 😏
                - paragraph [ref=e10789]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e10790]: My Assessment
                - paragraph [ref=e10791]: "The architecture is now at an unusual point:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10792]: Ontology ✓ Relationships ✓ Invariants ✓ Schemas ✓ Governance ✓
                - paragraph [ref=e10793]: Nothing fundamental appears unresolved.
                - paragraph [ref=e10794]: That's rare.
                - paragraph [ref=e10795]: Usually at this stage there is still a disguised contradiction hiding behind a friendly field name.
                - paragraph [ref=e10796]: I don't currently see one.
                - paragraph [ref=e10797]: "The next likely discoveries are implementation discoveries:"
                - paragraph [ref=e10798]: Registry topology Traversal mechanics Caching strategy Materialization rules Mode query surfaces
                - paragraph [ref=e10799]: Which is exactly where an architecture should be before somebody starts writing code and teaching the universe new and creative ways to throw exceptions. 🛰️

              - paragraph [ref=e10825]: The governance equivalent of Architectural Law 01. Every time process was attempted — voting, committees, approval chains — the solution collapsed. Every time topology was used — ownership, consequence maps, authority domains — the solution stabilized.

                  - text: ↓ Ownership ↓ Authority ↓ Consequences ↓ Governance
              - paragraph [ref=e10860]: Direction matters. Governance emerges from ownership upward. It is not imposed on ownership downward. That direction is why the system avoided committee logic — governance had no independent authority to impose. It could only formalize what ownership already implied.
              - separator [ref=e10861]
              - paragraph [ref=e10862]:
                - strong [ref=e10863]: Registry Topology Analysis — Session G.1 Agenda
              - paragraph [ref=e10864]: "The three-property classification you've identified will matter enormously to implementation:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10865]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e10867]:
                  - generic [ref=e10868]:

                - code [ref=e10878]:
                  - generic [ref=e10879]: Property Highest Value Registry
                  - text: ───────────────────────────────────────────── Dependency Density ProjectRegistry → most other registries reference it Mutation Frequency ObservationRegistry → highest write throughput → every witnessed event lands here Authority Sensitivity DiscoveryRegistry → REVEALS controls visibility → confidence gates truth claims → corruption propagates furthest
              - paragraph [ref=e10880]: These are orthogonal properties. The registry that writes most is not the registry whose failure hurts most. The registry most referenced is not the registry most written. Implementation will care about all three independently.
              - paragraph [ref=e10881]:

                  - text: Owned nodes Owned edges Incoming dependencies (who references this registry) Outgoing dependencies (who this registry references) Write authority (what can be mutated here) Read authority (what can be queried here) Mutation frequency (expected throughput class) Failure impact (what breaks if this registry is unavailable) Invariants owned Migration sensitivity (constitutional vs schema changes likely)

                  - generic [ref=e10915]: DISCOVERED
                  - text: Ontology 10 nodes, 3 resolutions (Event, Deployment, Hypothesis) Relationships 9 edges, 3 classes, edge payloads, lifecycle classification Registries 10 domain + Graph Layer + Invariant Layer Invariants 7 named (INV-01 through INV-07), 4 engine taxonomy Schemas 10 node schemas, 9 schema laws, 2 architectural laws Governance 5 governance laws, migration window model, consequence map PRINCIPLES Observatory Existence Principle Fact / Finding Separation Evidence vs Authority Pipelines Observation Constitution — Article 1 Node Necessity Test (bidirectional) Citation Test Reconstruction Test Metric Test Field Costume Test Payload Test Registry Ownership Test REMAINING Session G.1 Registry Topology Analysis Session H Content Schema Spec (formalization) Session I Cross-Link System Spec App Specs Three mode surfaces against real architecture
              - separator [ref=e10916]
              - paragraph [ref=e10917]:
                - strong [ref=e10918]: The Honest Assessment
              - paragraph [ref=e10919]: The architecture has no visible disguised contradictions remaining. That is genuinely unusual at this stage. It happened because every principle was pressure-tested rather than assumed, every field was challenged before it was admitted, and the graph was allowed to steal responsibilities from the schema whenever it could do so more honestly.
              - paragraph [ref=e10920]: The next discoveries will be implementation discoveries. Registry topology under real query load. Traversal mechanics under real graph depth. Caching strategy when ObservationRegistry throughput meets DiscoveryRegistry authority sensitivity.
              - paragraph [ref=e10921]: Those are solvable problems. They are not architectural unknowns.
              - paragraph [ref=e10922]: The difference between an architectural unknown and an implementation problem is that implementation problems have the courtesy to announce themselves clearly when the first exception is thrown. Architectural unknowns wait six months and then arrive quietly at 2AM wearing a disguise.
              - paragraph [ref=e10923]: The Observatory appears to have run out of disguises. 🛰️

              - separator [ref=e10969]

              - blockquote [ref=e10972]:
                - paragraph [ref=e10973]: Dependency density and authority are orthogonal.
              - paragraph [ref=e10974]: The registry most referenced is not the registry with highest authority. The registry most written is not the registry whose failure hurts most. Conflating these produces architectures that protect the wrong things.
              - separator [ref=e10975]
              - paragraph [ref=e10976]:

                - code [ref=e10991]:
                  - generic [ref=e10992]: Dependency Density How many registries reference me?
                  - text: Mutation Frequency How often am I written? Authority Sensitivity How dangerous is incorrect data here? Blast Radius How much degrades if I fail?
              - paragraph [ref=e10993]: Four distinct properties. Four distinct engineering responses. A single criticality score would lie by averaging them into false equivalence.
              - separator [ref=e10994]
              - paragraph [ref=e10995]:
                - strong [ref=e10996]: "Pressure Test: ObservationRegistry Blast Radius"
              - paragraph [ref=e10997]: "The table assigns Blast Radius: High to ObservationRegistry. The document then argues you can survive temporarily without new observations. Those two claims are in tension."
              - paragraph [ref=e10998]: "Resolve by distinguishing failure modes:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e10999]:

                  - generic [ref=e11013]: "ObservationRegistry write failure:"
                  - text: "New observations stop arriving Existing graph remains intact Discovery, Pattern, Explorer all continue on existing data Blast Radius: MEDIUM (degraded input, not degraded structure) ObservationRegistry read failure or corruption: Evidence chains become unqueryable Discovery admissibility checks fail Pattern derivation halts Self-audit breaks Blast Radius: VERY HIGH"
              - paragraph [ref=e11014]: Write failure and read/corruption failure have different blast radii. The table needs to distinguish them or it will mislead infrastructure decisions.

              - paragraph [ref=e11105]: "Two changes from the original:"
              - list [ref=e11106]:
                - listitem [ref=e11107]:
                  - text: TradeoffRegistry Density corrected to Low — Tradeoffs are
                  - emphasis [ref=e11108]: referenced
                  - text: by few registries even though they carry high semantic weight. Density and value-per-node are orthogonal, which is exactly the point being made.
                - listitem [ref=e11109]: ObservationRegistry Blast Radius split.
              - separator [ref=e11110]
              - paragraph [ref=e11111]:
                - strong [ref=e11112]: The Infrastructure Foresight — Named
              - paragraph [ref=e11113]: "Session E made a decision that accidentally protected ObservationRegistry throughput before the throughput problem was visible:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e11114]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e11116]:
                  - generic [ref=e11117]:
                    - img [ref=e11119]
                    - img [ref=e11123]
                - code [ref=e11127]:
                  - generic [ref=e11128]: "Session E decision: Validation Engine synchronous"
                  - text: "Everything else reactive Session G.1 consequence: Observation write → Validation only (synchronous) → Discovery recalculation (reactive) → Pattern recalculation (reactive) → Consistency audit (reactive) → Activation updates (reactive)"
              - paragraph [ref=e11129]: The alternative — full synchronous cascade on every Observation write — would have made ObservationRegistry's Very High mutation frequency architecturally catastrophic. Session E closed that failure mode seven sessions before the failure mode was identified.
              - paragraph [ref=e11130]: "This deserves a name:"
              - paragraph [ref=e11131]:
                - strong [ref=e11132]: "Architectural Foresight Instance 01:"
              - blockquote [ref=e11133]:
                - paragraph [ref=e11134]: The synchronous/reactive split in the Invariant Layer protects ObservationRegistry write throughput as a side effect of authority modeling.
              - paragraph [ref=e11135]: Not designed. Discovered. The principles protected the infrastructure before the infrastructure was considered.
              - separator [ref=e11136]
              - paragraph [ref=e11137]:
                - strong [ref=e11138]: The Three Critical Registry Types — Locked
              - group "Code" [ref=e11139]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e11141]:
                  - generic [ref=e11142]:
                    - img [ref=e11144]
                    - img [ref=e11148]
                - code [ref=e11152]:
                  - generic [ref=e11153]: Throughput Critical ObservationRegistry
                  - text: Highest write volume Architecture must protect write path Authority Critical DiscoveryRegistry Controls visibility via REVEALS Corruption propagates furthest into Explorer/Engineer Knowledge Density Critical TradeoffRegistry Highest value-per-node ratio Losing one node loses an entire decision chain Backup priority disproportionate to volume Connectivity Critical ProjectRegistry Most referenced node type Graph center of gravity Unavailability disconnects most traversal paths
              - paragraph [ref=e11154]: "Four forms of criticality. Four different infrastructure responses:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e11155]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e11157]:
                  - generic [ref=e11158]:
                    - img [ref=e11160]
                    - img [ref=e11164]
                - code [ref=e11168]:
                  - generic [ref=e11169]: Throughput Critical → write path optimization, async cascade
                  - text: Authority Critical → corruption detection, audit priority Knowledge Density → backup frequency disproportionate to write volume Connectivity Critical → availability SLA, graceful degradation paths
              - separator [ref=e11170]
              - paragraph [ref=e11171]:
                - strong [ref=e11172]: What Session G.1 Just Produced
              - paragraph [ref=e11173]: "The topology analysis is complete enough to make infrastructure decisions. Not implementation decisions — those require load numbers the architecture doesn't have yet. But infrastructure decisions: what to protect first, what failure modes matter most, where to invest in redundancy versus throughput."
              - paragraph [ref=e11174]: The architecture has stopped being philosophy. It's started predicting engineering problems before engineering begins.
              - paragraph [ref=e11175]: That's the moment an abstract design becomes a real one. 🛰️

              - paragraph [ref=e11264]:

                  - text: Write failure → Medium Read failure → Very High Corruption → Catastrophic
              - paragraph [ref=e11283]: Corruption is categorically different from absence. The Observatory built fifty sessions of machinery to distinguish those two things. Registry Law 02 ensures infrastructure treats them differently too.

              - generic [ref=e11304]:
                - paragraph [ref=e11305]: I am absolutely in the goblin phase. 🧌
                - paragraph [ref=e11306]: "The good news: this is the productive goblin phase."
                - paragraph [ref=e11307]: "The bad news: normal humans stopped following this conversation somewhere around Session B when \"Event was compressed into Observation.type\" and never returned."
                - paragraph [ref=e11308]: "What you're experiencing is a very recognizable architecture phenomenon:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11309]: "Phase 1: Learning \"Interesting.\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11310]: "Phase 2: Understanding \"That makes sense.\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11311]: "Phase 3: Internalization \"Obviously.\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11312]: "Phase 4: Goblin \"Why would anyone ever do it differently?\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11313]: You're somewhere between Phase 3 and Phase 4.
                - paragraph [ref=e11314]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11315]: The Real Signal
                - paragraph [ref=e11316]: "A few weeks ago:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11317]: Observation Discovery Pattern Registry Invariant
                - paragraph [ref=e11318]: were concepts.
                - paragraph [ref=e11319]: Now they're landmarks.
                - paragraph [ref=e11320]: "You don't think:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11321]: "\"PatternRegistry owns DERIVED_FROM\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11322]: "You think:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11323]: "\"Of course it does. Where else would it live?\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11324]: That transition is important.
                - paragraph [ref=e11325]: It means the architecture has become compressible in your head.
                - paragraph [ref=e11326]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11327]: The Hidden Test
                - paragraph [ref=e11328]: "A surprisingly reliable test is:"
                - blockquote [ref=e11329]:
                  - paragraph [ref=e11330]: Can you predict the answer before the pressure test is performed?
                - paragraph [ref=e11331]: "Early sessions looked like:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11332]: Question ↓ Reasoning ↓ Answer
                - paragraph [ref=e11333]: "Recent sessions look like:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11334]: Question ↓ Gut feeling ↓ Pressure test ↓ Gut feeling was correct
                - paragraph [ref=e11335]: "Examples:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11336]: EvidenceLink Node? "Probably not."
                - paragraph [ref=e11337]: "Pressure test: Not navigable."
                - paragraph [ref=e11338]: "Result: Rejected."
                - paragraph [ref=e11339]: audience field? "Feels wrong."
                - paragraph [ref=e11340]: "Pressure test: Presentation disguised as content."
                - paragraph [ref=e11341]: "Result: Rejected."
                - paragraph [ref=e11342]: researchObjective? "Feels redundant."
                - paragraph [ref=e11343]: "Pressure test: Summary + graph already own it."
                - paragraph [ref=e11344]: "Result: Rejected."
                - paragraph [ref=e11345]: You're no longer discovering answers.
                - paragraph [ref=e11346]: You're discovering why the answer was already obvious.
                - paragraph [ref=e11347]: That is textbook terrain familiarity.
                - paragraph [ref=e11348]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11349]: The Funny Part
                - paragraph [ref=e11350]: The architecture itself predicted this outcome.
                - paragraph [ref=e11351]: "Look at what we've built:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11352]: Observation ↓ Discovery ↓ Pattern
                - paragraph [ref=e11353]: "At the beginning:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11354]: You had Observations.
                - paragraph [ref=e11355]: "Then:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11356]: You had Discoveries.
                - paragraph [ref=e11357]: "Now:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11358]: You have Patterns.
                - paragraph [ref=e11359]: You're literally running the Observatory's own epistemology on the Observatory.
                - paragraph [ref=e11360]: The architecture taught itself to you using the same pipeline it uses to model reality.
                - paragraph [ref=e11361]: That's either beautifully recursive or a warning sign. Hard to tell. 😑
                - paragraph [ref=e11362]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11363]: What Happens Next
                - paragraph [ref=e11364]: The next stage is dangerous.
                - paragraph [ref=e11365]: Not because the architecture is fragile.
                - paragraph [ref=e11366]: Because everything starts feeling obvious.
                - paragraph [ref=e11367]: When that happens humans become confident enough to skip writing things down.
                - paragraph [ref=e11368]: "Then six months later:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11369]: "Future Goblin: \"Why did we do it this way?\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11370]: "Past Goblin: \"Because it was obvious.\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11371]: "Future Goblin: \"Helpful. Thanks.\""
                - paragraph [ref=e11372]: Which is why the next step is almost certainly not more architecture.
                - paragraph [ref=e11373]: It's formalization.
                - paragraph [ref=e11374]: Registry Architecture Spec Content Schema Spec Cross-Link Spec Invariant Spec
                - paragraph [ref=e11375]: Boring.
                - paragraph [ref=e11376]: Necessary.
                - paragraph [ref=e11377]: The architectural equivalent of labeling drawers before you forget what's inside them.
                - paragraph [ref=e11378]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11379]: "My assessment:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11380]: "Architecture Maturity: High"
                - paragraph [ref=e11381]: "Internal Consistency: High"
                - paragraph [ref=e11382]: "Known Contradictions: None visible"
                - paragraph [ref=e11383]: "Implementation Risk: Moderate"
                - paragraph [ref=e11384]: "Architectural Risk: Surprisingly low"
                - paragraph [ref=e11385]: The goblin should be pleased.
                - paragraph [ref=e11386]: The next monster lurking in the cave is not ontology.
                - paragraph [ref=e11387]: It's implementation.
                - paragraph [ref=e11388]: And implementation has the courtesy to fail loudly instead of spending six months pretending everything is fine. 🛰️🧌
