                - paragraph [ref=e11485]: Enough of goblin, let's talk like civilized monsters. Civilized monsters it is. 🐉
                - paragraph [ref=e11486]: "We've crossed the point where this is architecture discussion and entered the phase ancient engineers would have recognized immediately:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11487]: The Map Exists. Now preserve it before memory mutates it.
                - paragraph [ref=e11488]: Humans call this documentation.
                - paragraph [ref=e11489]: "Monsters know the proper name:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11490]: Rune Carving.
                - paragraph [ref=e11491]: Because that's what we're actually doing.
                - paragraph [ref=e11492]: Not explaining.
                - paragraph [ref=e11493]: Not teaching.
                - paragraph [ref=e11494]: Not brainstorming.
                - paragraph [ref=e11495]: Carving discovered terrain into stone so Future Monster doesn't have to rediscover the same cave system with a flashlight and bad life choices.
                - paragraph [ref=e11496]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11497]: The recursion still amuses me though.
                - paragraph [ref=e11498]: "We accidentally built:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11499]: Observatory ↓ Observation ↓ Discovery ↓ Pattern
                - paragraph [ref=e11500]: "Then applied it to:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11501]: Observatory Architecture ↓ Architecture Observations ↓ Architectural Discoveries ↓ Architectural Patterns
                - paragraph [ref=e11502]: "Then applied that to governance:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11503]: Historical Validity ↓ Current Compliance ↓ Architectural Evolution
                - paragraph [ref=e11504]: The system survived self-application.
                - paragraph [ref=e11505]: That's the important part.
                - paragraph [ref=e11506]: Not because it's elegant.
                - paragraph [ref=e11507]: Because most systems explode right there.
                - paragraph [ref=e11508]: The first time a principle has to explain itself, it usually develops a nervous cough and starts making exceptions.
                - paragraph [ref=e11509]: The Observatory didn't.
                - paragraph [ref=e11510]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11511]: What fascinates me most isn't the nodes.
                - paragraph [ref=e11512]: It's the compression history.
                - paragraph [ref=e11513]: "If we wrote the archaeology of this thing, it would look like:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11514]: Extinct Species
                - paragraph [ref=e11515]: Event → Observation.type
                - paragraph [ref=e11516]: EvidenceLink → Evidentiary Edge

                - paragraph [ref=e11519]: audience → rejected
                - paragraph [ref=e11520]: special Experimental Platform capability → rejected
                - paragraph [ref=e11521]: affectedLayer → rejected
                - paragraph [ref=e11522]: severity → rejected
                - paragraph [ref=e11523]: impact → rejected
                - paragraph [ref=e11524]: Half the architecture is a graveyard of ideas that sounded reasonable for five minutes.
                - paragraph [ref=e11525]: That's usually a sign of progress.
                - paragraph [ref=e11526]: A bad architecture accumulates concepts.
                - paragraph [ref=e11527]: A good architecture accumulates tombstones.
                - paragraph [ref=e11528]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11529]: And honestly?
                - paragraph [ref=e11530]: The thing I'd be most worried about now isn't implementation.
                - paragraph [ref=e11531]: It's forgetting why decisions were made.
                - paragraph [ref=e11532]: "Because six months from now Future Monster is going to stare at:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11533]: "Tradeoff { rationale? }"
                - paragraph [ref=e11534]: "and ask:"
                - paragraph [ref=e11535]: Why does this field exist when everything else got compressed?
                - paragraph [ref=e11536]: If the answer isn't recorded, someone will remove it.
                - paragraph [ref=e11537]: Then six months later they'll discover the Reconstruction Test again.
                - paragraph [ref=e11538]: Then re-add it.
                - paragraph [ref=e11539]: Then rename it.
                - paragraph [ref=e11540]: Then congratulate themselves.
                - paragraph [ref=e11541]: The circle of software life continues. 🫠
                - paragraph [ref=e11542]: "---"
                - paragraph [ref=e11543]: So I think your five-document order is correct.
                - paragraph [ref=e11544]: Not because of dependency.
                - paragraph [ref=e11545]: Because of entropy.
                - list [ref=e11546]:
                  - listitem [ref=e11547]: Ontology + Relationship Catalog
                - list [ref=e11548]:
                  - listitem [ref=e11549]: Registry Architecture Spec

                  - listitem [ref=e11553]: Schema Spec
                - list [ref=e11554]:

                - paragraph [ref=e11556]: That's the order of least information loss.
                - paragraph [ref=e11557]: The first document captures what exists.
                - paragraph [ref=e11558]: Everything else depends on that.
                - paragraph [ref=e11559]: Without Doc 1, Future Monster inherits mythology.
                - paragraph [ref=e11560]: With Doc 1, Future Monster inherits terrain.
                - paragraph [ref=e11561]: And terrain is far more useful than legends, despite the strong monster tradition of hoarding both. 🐉🛰️

              - paragraph [ref=e11807]:
                - strong [ref=e11808]: "My honest suggestion:"
              - paragraph [ref=e11809]: Review the five docs first — specifically looking for anything that feels wrong when read cold, not in the heat of discovery. That's the real pressure test for the documentation.
              - paragraph [ref=e11810]: "Then the question to answer before App Specs is: do you want the Cross-Link System Spec first, or go straight to App Specs and let the cross-link rules emerge from the mode requirements?"
              - paragraph [ref=e11811]: "The argument for Cross-Link first: modes will make traversal assumptions. Better to define legal traversal before designing surfaces that depend on it."
              - paragraph [ref=e11812]: "The argument for App Specs first: modes will reveal which cross-link rules actually matter vs which ones we'd be specifying into the void."
              - paragraph [ref=e11813]:
                - text: "My read:"
                - strong [ref=e11814]: Cross-Link Spec first.
                - text: The wireframes are locked and detailed enough that App Specs will generate real traversal requirements quickly — and underdefined traversal rules are the kind of thing that looks fine until someone tries to implement Engineer Mode Level 3 and discovers the graph doesn't support the query they need.
              - paragraph [ref=e11815]: But that decision can wait until after review. 🐉

              - blockquote [ref=e11936]:

              - paragraph [ref=e11938]: "Before naming rules, I'd apply the same discipline that saved Session B: classify traversal types before defining individual paths."
              - paragraph [ref=e11939]: "My read is there are four distinct traversal classes:"

                  - generic [ref=e11954]: Class 1 Owned traversal
                  - text: Reading your own registry's nodes and edges No authority check needed Class 2 Reference traversal Following a reference edge to another registry's node Read permitted, write forbidden Class 3 Evidence traversal Following evidentiary edges across registry boundaries Requires admissibility check Class 4 Derived traversal Following computed relationships (REVEALS, strength) Requires visibility check
              - paragraph [ref=e11955]: Each class has different authority requirements, different check costs, and different failure modes.

            - generic [ref=e12006]:
              - paragraph [ref=e12007]: Locked. And the diagnostic test you used is the right one — each class should map to exactly one authority model. Four classes had one collision. Five classes have none.
              - separator [ref=e12008]
              - paragraph [ref=e12009]:
                - strong [ref=e12010]: The Separation Principle — Applied Again
              - paragraph [ref=e12011]: "This is the third time the Observatory has made the same move:"

                  - generic [ref=e12015]:

                - code [ref=e12025]:
                  - generic [ref=e12026]: "Session C: Semantic vs Evidentiary edges"
                  - text: "(same graph path, different authority) Session I: Justification vs Derivation vs Visibility traversal (same graph shape, different authority, different engine)"
              - paragraph [ref=e12027]: Architectural Law 01 tapping the glass is the correct instinct. When two things look the same topologically but answer different questions and involve different engines, they are different species regardless of how similar the path looks.
              - separator [ref=e12028]
              - paragraph [ref=e12029]:
                - strong [ref=e12030]: Five-Class Traversal Taxonomy — Locked

                - rowgroup [ref=e12033]:

                    - cell "Activation" [ref=e12070]
              - paragraph [ref=e12071]: One class. One authority. No mixing. The same discipline that produced the edge catalog in Session C.
              - separator [ref=e12072]
              - paragraph [ref=e12073]:
                - strong [ref=e12074]: What Session I Needs To Specify Per Class
              - paragraph [ref=e12075]: "Now that classes are locked, each one needs four things defined:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e12076]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e12078]:
                  - generic [ref=e12079]:
                    - img [ref=e12081]
                    - img [ref=e12085]
                - code [ref=e12089]:
                  - generic [ref=e12090]: 1. Check model What must be verified before traversal is permitted?
                  - text: 2. Failure modes What happens when the check fails? 3. Write boundary Is mutation ever permitted across this traversal? 4. Alias rules Are UI-facing alias traversals legal, and under what class?
              - paragraph [ref=e12091]: "The alias question is the one I'd flag early. Several edges have canonical direction and UI aliases:"
              - group "Code" [ref=e12092]:
                - button "Copy to clipboard" [ref=e12094]:
                  - generic [ref=e12095]:
                    - img [ref=e12097]
                    - img [ref=e12101]
                - code [ref=e12105]:
                  - generic [ref=e12106]: "Canonical: Constraint → AFFECTS → Project"
                  - text: "UI alias: Project → CONSTRAINED_BY → Constraint Canonical: Tradeoff → EMBODIED_BY → Project UI alias: Project → EMBODIES → Tradeoff"
              - paragraph [ref=e12107]: These aliases traverse the same edge in reverse. Under the five-class model, both directions are Class 2 (Reference). But the alias direction was never formally defined as legal — it was assumed as "traversal only." Session I should make that explicit rather than leave it as inherited assumption.
              - separator [ref=e12108]
              - paragraph [ref=e12109]:
                - strong [ref=e12110]: Opening Question for Session I
              - paragraph [ref=e12111]: "Before check models: there's a prior question worth settling."
              - blockquote [ref=e12112]:
                - paragraph [ref=e12113]: Does traversal class affect what a mode is allowed to request?
              - paragraph [ref=e12114]: Recruiter Mode probably never needs Class 3 (Justification). Explorer Mode probably never needs Class 4 (Derivation). Engineer Mode needs all five.
              - paragraph [ref=e12115]: If traversal class is mode-aware, the check model becomes simpler per mode — each mode only carries the checks it needs. If traversal class is mode-agnostic and modes just happen to use different classes, the check model is uniform but potentially more expensive for simpler modes.
              - paragraph [ref=e12116]: This feels like Session I's first fork. Which way does the Observatory lean? 🐉
